Diffusive Limit of the One-species Vlasov–Maxwell–Boltzmann System for Cutoff Hard Potentials
Weijun Wu † , Fujun Zhou ∗ , Weihua Gong ‡ and Yuan Xu §

Abstract
. Diffusive limit of the one-species Vlasov–Maxwell–Boltzmann system in perturbation framework still remains unsolved, due to the weaker time decay rate compared with the two-species Vlasov–Maxwell–Boltzmann system. By employing the weighted energy method with two newly introduced weight functions and some novel treatments, we solve this problem for the full range of cutoff hard potentials 0 ≤ γ ≤ 1 0\leq\gamma\leq 1 . Uniform estimate with respect to the Knudsen number ε ∈ ( 0 , 1 ] \varepsilon\in(0,1] is established globally in time, which eventually leads to the global existence of solutions to the one-species Vlasov–Maxwell–Boltzmann system and hydrodynamic limit to the incompressible Navier–Stokes–Fourier–Maxwell system. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first result on diffusive limit of the one-species Vlasov–Maxwell–Boltzmann system in perturbation framework. Mathematics Subject Classification (2020) : 35Q20; 35Q83 Keywords : Hydrodynamic limit; Vlasov–Maxwell–Boltzmann system; incompressible Navier–Stokes–Fourier–Maxwell system; weighted energy method.



1.
Introduction

1.1.
Description of the Problem

The Vlasov–Maxwell–Boltzmann (VMB, for short) system is an important model in plasma physics to describe the time evolution of dilute charged particles
under the influence of their self-consistent electromagnetic field. Despite its importance, diffusive limit to the fluid equations has not been completely solved so far, such as the scaled two-species VMB system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\varepsilon\partial_{t}F^{\varepsilon}_{+}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}F^{\varepsilon}_{+}+(\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}+v\times B^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla_{v}F^{\varepsilon}_{+}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}Q(F^{\varepsilon}_{+},F^{\varepsilon}_{+})+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}Q(F^{\varepsilon}_{+},F^{\varepsilon}_{-}),\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\varepsilon\partial_{t}F^{\varepsilon}_{-}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}F^{\varepsilon}_{-}-(\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}+v\times B^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla_{v}F^{\varepsilon}_{-}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}Q(F^{\varepsilon}_{-},F^{\varepsilon}_{-})+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}Q(F^{\varepsilon}_{-},F^{\varepsilon}_{+}),\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}-\nabla_{x}\times B^{\varepsilon}=-\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v(F^{\varepsilon}_{+}-F^{\varepsilon}_{-}){\mathrm{d}}v,\quad\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\cdot E^{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(F^{\varepsilon}_{+}-F^{\varepsilon}_{-}){\mathrm{d}}v,\\[7.11317pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}B^{\varepsilon}+\nabla_{x}\times E^{\varepsilon}=0,\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\;\,\nabla_{x}\cdot B^{\varepsilon}=0.\end{array}\right.

(1.1)

Here
F_{\pm}^{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)\geq 0
are the density distribution functions for the ions
(+)
and the electrons
(-)
, respectively, at time
t\geq 0
, position
x=(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
and velocity
v=(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}

[
39
]
.
The electromagnetic field
[E^{\varepsilon}(t,x),B^{\varepsilon}(t,x)]
is coupled with the density function
F^{\varepsilon}_{\pm}(t,x,v)
through the celebrated Maxwell system in (
1.1
).
The positive parameter
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
is the Knudsen number, which equals to the ratio of the mean free path to the macroscopic length scale.

In the physical situation, the ion mass is usually much
heavier than the electron mass, so that the electrons move faster than the ions.
Hence, the ions are often described by a fixed ion background
n_{b}(x)
, and only electrons move. For this simplified situation, the two-species VMB system (
1.1
) is reduced to the following scaled one-species VMB system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\varepsilon\partial_{t}F^{\varepsilon}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}F^{\varepsilon}+\left(\varepsilon^{2}E^{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon v\times B^{\varepsilon}\right)\cdot\nabla_{v}F^{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}Q(F^{\varepsilon},F^{\varepsilon}),\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}-\nabla_{x}\times B^{\varepsilon}=-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}vF^{\varepsilon}{\mathrm{d}}v,\qquad\;\par\nabla_{x}\cdot E^{\varepsilon}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}F^{\varepsilon}{\mathrm{d}}v-n_{b},\\[8.53581pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}B^{\varepsilon}+\nabla_{x}\times E^{\varepsilon}=0,\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\par\nabla_{x}\cdot B^{\varepsilon}=0.\end{array}\right.

(1.2)

Here,
F^{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)\geq 0
represents the density of the electrons at time
t\geq 0
, velocity
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}

and position
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, and the fixed constant ion background charge
n_{b}>0
is scaled to
1
.
The initial data of the VMB system (
1.2
) are imposed as

\displaystyle F^{\varepsilon}(0,x,v)=F_{0}^{\varepsilon}(x,v),\quad E^{\varepsilon}(0,x)=E^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x),\quad B^{\varepsilon}(0,x)=B^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x)

(1.3)

with the compatibility conditions

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\cdot E_{0}^{\varepsilon}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}F^{\varepsilon}_{0}{\mathrm{d}}v-1,\quad\nabla_{x}\cdot B_{0}^{\varepsilon}=0.

The Boltzmann collision operator
Q(G_{1},G_{2})
in (
1.2
)
is defined as

\displaystyle Q(G_{1},G_{2})(v)

\displaystyle:=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}q_{0}(\theta)\big[G_{1}(v^{\prime})G_{2}(v_{*}^{\prime})-G_{1}(v)G_{2}(v_{*})\big]{\mathrm{d}}v_{*}{\mathrm{d}}\omega,

where
v^{\prime}=v-[(v-v_{*})\cdot\omega]\omega
and
v_{*}^{\prime}=v_{*}+[(v-v_{*})\cdot\omega]\omega
by the conservation of momentum and energy

v^{\prime}+v_{*}^{\prime}=v+v_{*},\quad|v^{\prime}|^{2}+|v_{*}^{\prime}|^{2}=|v|^{2}+|v_{*}|^{2}.

The collision kernel
|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}q_{0}(\theta)
, depending only on

|v-v_{*}|
and
\cos\theta=\omega\cdot\frac{v-v_{*}}{|v-v_{*}|}
, satisfies
-3<\gamma\leq 1
and the Grad’s
angular cutoff assumption

0\leq q_{0}(\theta)\leq C|\cos\theta|.

The exponent
\gamma
is determined by the potential of intermolecular forces, and is classified into the
soft potential case when
-3<\gamma<0
and the hard potential case when
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
. In particular, the hard potential
case includes the hard sphere model with
\gamma=1,q_{0}(\theta)=C|\cos\theta|
and the Maxwell model with
\gamma=0
.

Much significant progress has been made on global well-posedness of the standard VMB system, including the two-species VMB system (
1.1
) and
the one-species VMB system (
1.2
) with
\varepsilon=1
. Firstly, for the standard two-species VMB system (
1.1
) with
\varepsilon=1
, a significant breakthrough
was achieved by
[
21
]
, wherein Guo constructed classical solutions in perturbation framework for the hard sphere case
\gamma=1
in
\mathbb{T}^{3}
by employing the robust high-order energy method. Subsequently, Strain and Duan–Strain
[
14
,
41
]
established global existence and optimal time decay rate of classical solutions for the hard sphere case
\gamma=1
in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Ultimately, global existence for the full range of soft potentials
-3<\gamma<0
was solved by
[
12
]
. Secondly, for the standard one-species VMB system (
1.2
) with
\varepsilon=1
, the mathematical theory is much more delicate than the two-species VMB system, due to the slow time decay rate of solutions to the one-species VMB system.
The global existence of classical solutions in perturbation framework is obtained by Duan
[
11
]
for the hard sphere case
\gamma=1
. Then
the global existence and time decay estimates of classical solutions for the soft potential case
-1<\gamma<0
was solved by
[
34
]
.
In addition, Li–Yang–Zhong
[
36
]
utilized spectral analysis method to study global solutions and optimal time decay rate of the VMB system with
the hard sphere case
\gamma=1
for both two-species and one-species. Moreover, Arsénio–Saint-Raymond
[
2
]
obtained global
renormalized solutions of the VMB system with general large initial data for both two-species and one-species.
Except for the progress on the cutoff VMB system described above, the standard non-cutoff VMB system also received some research,
such as
[
13
]
for soft potentials
\max\{-3,-\frac{3}{2}-2s\}<\gamma<-2s
with strong angular singularity
\frac{1}{2}\leq s<1
and
[
18
]
for soft potentials
\max\{-3,-\frac{3}{2}-2s\}<\gamma<-2s
with weak angular singularity
0<s<\frac{1}{2}
.

Hydrodynamic limit of the VMB system has also been the focus of extensive research efforts.
In a recent notable development, Arsénio–Saint-Raymond
[
2
]
justified various limits (depending on the scalings) towards incompressible viscous electro-magneto-hydrodynamics in the framework of renormalized solutions. Among these limits, the most two critical cases are the two-fluid incompressible Navier–Stokes–Fourier–Maxwell (NSFM, for short) system with Ohm’s law from the two-species VMB system (
1.1
) and the incompressible NSFM system from the one-species VMB system (
1.2
). This fundamental work opened up a series of subsequent progress on the study of the hydrodynamic limit of the VMB system, mainly for the two-species
VMB system in perturbation framework. Jiang–Luo
[
30
]
justified the two-fluid incompressible NSFM limit of (
1.1
) in perturbation framework for the cutoff hard sphere case
\gamma=1
, and also considered Hilbert expansion
[
7
]
for the cutoff hard potential case
0\leq\gamma\leq 1

[
31
]
. Recently, Jiang–Lei
[
29
]
investigated the two-fluid incompressible NSFM limit for the cutoff soft potentials
-1\leq\gamma<0
and the non-cutoff soft potentials
\max\{-3,-\frac{3}{2}-2s\}<\gamma<-2s
with strong angular singularity
\frac{1}{2}\leq s<1
. Then this two-fluid incompressible NSFM limit was extended to the non-cutoff potentials
\gamma>\max\{-3,-\frac{3}{2}-2s\}
with full range
0<s<1

[
45
]
.
Additionally, Yang–Zhong
[
46
]
demonstrated the above limit and the convergence rate for the cutoff hard sphere case
\gamma=1
by spectral analysis method.
In addition to the incompressible NSFM limit of the two-species VMB system mentioned above, Jang
[
28
]
studied the incompressible Vlasov–Navier–Stokes–Fourier limit for the cutoff hard sphere case
\gamma=1
in
\mathbb{T}^{3}
.
However, for the hydrodynamic limit of the one-species VMB system in perturbation framework, the research so far have only focused on the compressible
Euler–Maxwell limit in hyperbolic regime, as presented by
[
15
]
for the cutoff hard sphere
case
\gamma=1
and
[
33
]
for the non-cutoff soft potential case
\max\{-3,-\frac{3}{2}-2s\}<\gamma<-2s
with
0<s<1
, respectively.
For more related works on hydrodynamic limit of relevant kinetic
equations, we refer to
[
3
,
4
,
5
,
8
,
10
,
17
,
19
,
20
,
23
,
24
,
25
,
27
,
32
,
35
,
37
,
40
,
44
]
and the references cited therein.

As indicated from the aforementioned research progress,
the existing results on hydrodynamic limit of the one-species VMB system in perturbation framework are all limited to the hyperbolic limit toward the Euler–Maxwell system
[
15
,
33
]
,
whereas diffusive limit of the one-species VMB system still remains open.
The purpose of this paper is to fill in this gap and investigate
diffusive limit of the one-species VMB system (
1.2
)–(
1.3
) for cutoff hard potentials
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
.
In fact, diffusive limit of the one-species VMB system (
1.2
)–(
1.3
) is more difficult than
the two-species VMB system, due to the weak time decay rate of solutions.
By employing the weighted energy estimates with two newly introduced weight functions as well as some novel treatments, we
overcome the difficulty of slow time decay rate of solutions and establish the uniform estimate in
\varepsilon\in(0,1]

globally in time, which eventually
leads to the convergence of the VMB system to the incompressible NSFM system for the full range of cutoff hard potentials.
This firstly justifies diffusive limit of classical solutions to the one-species Vlasov–Maxwell–Boltzmann system.

1.2.
Reformulation of the Problem

We investigate diffusive limit of the one-species VMB system (
1.2
) in perturbation framework around the global Maxwellian

\mu=\mu(v):=(2\pi)^{-3/2}\mathrm{e}^{-|v|^{2}/2}.

By writing

F^{\varepsilon}(t,x,v)=\mu+\varepsilon\mu^{1/2}f^{\varepsilon}(t,x,v),\quad F^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x,v)=\mu+\varepsilon\mu^{1/2}f_{0}^{\varepsilon}(x,v)

with the fluctuations
f^{\varepsilon}
and
f_{0}^{\varepsilon}
,
the VMB system
(
1.2
)–(
1.3
) is transformed into the following equivalent perturbed VMB system

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\!\!\!\partial_{t}f^{\varepsilon}\!+\!\frac{1}{\varepsilon}v\!\cdot\nabla_{x}{f}^{\varepsilon}\!+\!(\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\!+\!v\times B^{\varepsilon})\!\cdot\nabla_{v}{f}^{\varepsilon}\!-\!E^{\varepsilon}\!\cdot\!v\mu^{1/2}\!+\!\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}L{f}^{\varepsilon}=\!\varepsilon\displaystyle\!\frac{v}{2}\cdot E^{\varepsilon}{f}^{\varepsilon}\!+\!\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Gamma({f}^{\varepsilon}\!,\!{f}^{\varepsilon}),\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\!\!\!\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}-\nabla_{x}\times B^{\varepsilon}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v\mu^{1/2}f^{\varepsilon}{\mathrm{d}}v,\qquad\qquad\;\nabla_{x}\cdot E^{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mu^{1/2}f^{\varepsilon}{\mathrm{d}}v,\\[8.53581pt]
\displaystyle\!\!\!\partial_{t}B^{\varepsilon}+\nabla_{x}\times E^{\varepsilon}=0,\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\;\;\;\nabla_{x}\cdot B^{\varepsilon}=0,\\[8.53581pt]
\displaystyle\!\!\!f^{\varepsilon}(0,x,v)=f^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x,v),\qquad E^{\varepsilon}(0,x)=E^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x),\quad\;B^{\varepsilon}(0,x)=B^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x),\end{array}\right.

where the linearized Boltzmann collision operator
Lf^{\varepsilon}
and the nonlinear collision term
\Gamma(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon})
are respectively defined by

\displaystyle Lf^{\varepsilon}\;

\displaystyle:=-\mu^{-1/2}\left[Q(\mu,\mu^{1/2}f^{\varepsilon})+Q(\mu^{1/2}f^{\varepsilon},\mu)\right]\equiv\nu f-Kf,

\displaystyle\Gamma(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon})\;

\displaystyle:=\mu^{-1/2}Q(\mu^{1/2}f^{\varepsilon},\mu^{1/2}f^{\varepsilon}).

Here,
\nu=\nu(v)
is the collision frequency and
K
is a compact operator on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3})
, given by

\begin{split}\nu\equiv\nu(v)&:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}Q_{-}(\sqrt{\mu},\mu)=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}q_{0}(\theta)\mu(v_{*})\mathrm{d}\omega\mathrm{d}v_{*},\\
Kf&:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}\Big[Q_{+}(\mu,\sqrt{\mu}f)+Q_{+}(\sqrt{\mu}f,\mu)-Q_{-}(\mu,\sqrt{\mu}f)\Big].\end{split}

(1.8)

It is well-known that the operator
L
is non-negative with null space

\mathrm{N}(L)=\text{span}\Big\{\mu^{1/2},\ v\mu^{1/2},\ \frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\mu^{1/2}\Big\}

cf.
[
8
]
.
For given function
f(t,x,v)
, the following macro-micro decomposition was introduced in
[
22
]

f=\mathbf{P}f+(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f.

(1.9)

Here,
\mathbf{P}
denotes the orthogonal projection from
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3})
to
\mathcal{N}(L)
, defined by

\mathbf{P}f:=\Big[a(t,x)+v\cdot b(t,x)+\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}c(t,x)\Big]\mu^{1/2}.

(1.10)

Furthermore, it is well-known that there exists a positive constant

\sigma_{0}>0
such that

\langle Lf,f\rangle_{L_{x,v}^{2}}\geq\sigma_{0}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}(\nu)}^{2}.

(1.11)

1.3.
Notations

Throughout the paper,
C
denotes a generic positive constant independent of
\varepsilon
.
We use
X\lesssim Y
to denote
X\leq CY
, where
C
is a constant independent of
X
,
Y
. We also use the notation
X\approx Y
to represent
X\lesssim Y
and
Y\lesssim X
. The notation
X\ll 1
means that
X
is a positive constant small enough.

The multi-indexs
\alpha=[\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}]\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
and
\beta=[\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\beta_{3}]\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
will be used to record space and velocity derivatives, respectively. We denote the
\alpha
-th order space partial derivatives by
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}=\partial_{x_{1}}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial_{x_{2}}^{\alpha_{2}}\partial_{x_{3}}^{\alpha_{3}}
,
and the
\beta
-th order velocity partial derivatives by
\partial_{v}^{\beta}=\partial_{v_{1}}^{\beta_{1}}\partial_{v_{2}}^{\beta_{2}}\partial_{v_{3}}^{\beta_{3}}
.
In addition,
\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}=\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\partial^{\beta}_{v}=\partial^{\alpha_{1}}_{x_{1}}\partial^{\alpha_{2}}_{x_{2}}\partial^{\alpha_{3}}_{x_{3}}\partial^{\beta_{1}}_{v_{1}}\partial^{\beta_{2}}_{v_{2}}\partial^{\beta_{3}}_{v_{3}}
stands for the mixed space-velocity derivative.
The length of
\alpha
is denoted by
|\alpha|=\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}
.
If each component of
\theta
is not greater than that of
\bar{\theta}
, we denote by
\theta\leq\bar{\theta}
.
\theta<\bar{\theta}
means
\theta\leq\bar{\theta}
and
|\theta|<|\bar{\theta}|
.
In addition, the notation
\nabla_{x}^{k}=\partial^{\alpha}_{x}
will be used when
\beta=0,|\alpha|=k
.

We use
|\cdot|_{L^{p}_{v}}
to denote the
L^{p}
norm in
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, and use
\|\cdot\|_{L^{q}_{x,v}}
to denote the
L_{x,v}^{q}
-norm on the variables

(x,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
for
1\leq q\leq\infty
. For an integer
m\geq 1
, we also
employ
\|\cdot\|_{W^{m,q}_{x,v}}
to represent the norm of the Sobolev space

W^{m,q}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
. In particular, if
q=2
then
\|\cdot\|_{H^{m}_{x,v}}
stands for
the norm of
H^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})=W^{m,2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Similarly,

\|\cdot\|_{L^{q}_{x}}
,
\|\cdot\|_{H^{m}_{x}}
,
\|\cdot\|_{W^{m,q}_{x}}
and

\|\cdot\|_{H^{m}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}
stand for
the norms of the function spaces
L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})
,
H^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})
,

W^{m,q}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x})
and
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v},H^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}))
, respectively.
Besides,
\|\cdot\|_{L^{p}_{x}L^{q}_{v}}
denotes
\||\cdot|_{L^{q}_{v}}\|_{L^{p}_{x}}
.
The norm of a vector means the sum of the norms for all components
of this vector.

Let
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}

\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{L^{2}_{x}}
,
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}
denote the
L^{2}_{v}
inner product in
\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}
, the
L^{2}_{x}
inner product in
\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}
,
and the
L^{2}_{x,v}
inner product in
\mathbb{R}_{x,v}^{3}
, respectively.
We also define the weighted
L^{2}_{x,v}
and
H^{N}_{x}L^{2}_{v}
space endowed with the norms

\displaystyle\|g\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle=\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nu(v)|g(x,v)|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v,\quad\|g\|^{2}_{H^{N}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq N}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}g\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)},

respectively, where
\nu=\nu(v)
will be given in (
1.8
).

Finally,
we use
\Lambda^{-s}f(x)
to denote

\displaystyle\Lambda^{-s}f(x):={(2\pi)^{-3/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|y|^{-s}\widehat{f}(y)e^{ix\cdot y}{\mathrm{d}}y,\qquad s\in(0,3)

where
\widehat{f}(y):={(2\pi)^{-3/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f(x)e^{-ix\cdot y}{\mathrm{d}}x
represents the Fourier transform of
f(x)
.

1.4.
Main Results

To state the main results of this paper, we introduce the following fundamental instant energy functional

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{N}(t)\sim\;

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|\leq N}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq N}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq N}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}},

(1.12)

and the corresponding dissipation rate functional

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{D}}_{N}(t)\sim\;&\!\!\sum_{1\leq|\alpha|\leq N}\!\!\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\!+\!\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq N}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\mathbf{I}\!-\!\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\varepsilon^{2}\sum_{1\leq|\alpha|\leq N-1}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&+\varepsilon^{2}\sum_{2\leq|\alpha|\leq N-1}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}},\end{split}

(1.13)

where the integer
N\in\mathbb{Z}
will be determined later.

On the one hand, owing to the weaker dissipation of the linearized Boltzmann operator
L
for hard potentials rather than the hard sphere case, in order to deal with the Lorentz force term brought by the electromagnetic field, we introduce the following time-velocity weight function

\displaystyle\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(\alpha,\beta)

\displaystyle:=\langle v\rangle^{l_{1}-4|\alpha|-4|\beta|}e^{\frac{q\langle v\rangle^{2}}{(1+t)^{\vartheta}}},

(1.14)

where
\langle v\rangle:=\sqrt{1+|v|^{2}}
and the constants
l_{1},q,\vartheta\geq 0
will be determined later. Moreover,
sometimes we write
\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(|\alpha|,|\beta|)
to denote
\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(\alpha,\beta)
for simplicity.
Then the weighted instant energy functional
{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)

and the corresponding dissipation rate functional
{\overline{\mathcal{D}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)
are defined respectively as

\displaystyle{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t):=\;

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq N-1\end{subarray}}\left\|\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}},

(1.15)

\displaystyle{\overline{\mathcal{D}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t):=\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq N-1\end{subarray}}\left\|\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq N-1\end{subarray}}\frac{q\vartheta}{(1+t)^{1+\vartheta}}\left\|\langle v\rangle\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}.

(1.16)

On the other hand, comparing the instant energy functional (
1.12
) with dissipation rate functional (
1.13
), one can find that the dissipations of electromagnetic field is coupled with
\varepsilon^{2}
, implying that the slow time decay rate of the one-species VMB system can not close the
\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(\alpha,\beta)
-weighted energy estimate. This urges us to introduce the second time-velocity weight function

\displaystyle\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\;

\displaystyle:=\langle v\rangle^{l_{2}-4|\alpha|-\frac{7}{2}|\beta|}e^{\frac{q\langle v\rangle^{2}}{(1+t)^{\vartheta}}},

(1.17)

where the constants
l_{2},q,\vartheta\geq 0
will be determined later. Similarly,
sometimes we write
\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(|\alpha|,|\beta|)
to denote
\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)
for simplicity.
Then the weighted
instant energy functional
{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}_{N,l_{2}}(t)
and the corresponding dissipation rate functional
{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}}_{N,l_{2}}(t)
are
respectively defined as

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}_{N,l_{2}}(t):=\;

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq N-1}(1+t)^{-|\beta|\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}}\left\|\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle+(1+t)^{-\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|+|\beta|=N,\\
|\alpha|\neq N\end{subarray}}(1+t)^{-|\beta|\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}}\left\|\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle+(1+t)^{-\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}}\varepsilon\sum_{|\alpha|=N}\left\|\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,0)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}},

(1.18)

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}}_{N,l_{2}}(t):=\;

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq N-1}(1+t)^{-|\beta|\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}}\frac{q\vartheta}{(1+t)^{1+\vartheta}}\left\|\langle v\rangle\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{0\leq|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq N-1}(1+t)^{-|\beta|\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\|\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle+(1+t)^{-\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq N,\\
|\alpha|\neq N\end{subarray}}(1+t)^{-|\beta|\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}}\frac{q\vartheta}{(1+t)^{1+\vartheta}}\left\|\langle v\rangle\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle+(1+t)^{-\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|+|\beta|=N,\\
|\alpha|\neq N\end{subarray}}(1+t)^{-|\beta|\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\|\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle+(1+t)^{-\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}}\sum_{|\alpha|=N}\frac{\varepsilon q\vartheta}{(1+t)^{1+\vartheta}}\left\|\langle v\rangle\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,0)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle+(1+t)^{-\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}}\sum_{|\alpha|=N}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\|\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,0)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}.

(1.19)

Moreover, a sufficient time decay rate is necessary to close the energy estimate. Due to the slow time decay rate of the linearized system of (
1.2
),
the time decay estimate of the nonlinear problem can not be obtained by the Duhamel principle and semigroup estimate of the linearized problem. Thus,
we have to resort to the Sobolev space with negative index and introduce the following instant energy functional
{\mathcal{E}}_{-s}(t)
and
the corresponding dissipation rate functional
{\mathcal{D}}_{-s}(t)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{-s}(t)\!:=\!\;

\displaystyle\left\|\Lambda^{-s}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\!+\!\left\|\Lambda^{-s}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\left\|\Lambda^{-s}B^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}},

(1.20)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{-s}(t)\!:=\!\;

\displaystyle\|\Lambda^{1-s}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\!\!+\!\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}\!-\!\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}^{2}\!+\varepsilon^{2}\|\Lambda^{1-s}E^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\!+\varepsilon^{2}\|\Lambda^{2-s}\!B^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}},

(1.21)

respectively, where the positive constant
s>0
will be determined later.

Besides, we introduce the following energy functional with the lowest
k
th order space derivative
\mathcal{E}^{k}_{N_{0}}(t)
and the corresponding dissipation rate functional with the lowest
k
-order space derivative
{\mathcal{D}}^{k}_{N_{0}}(t)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{k}_{N_{0}}(t)\sim\;

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leq|\alpha|\leq N_{0}}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\sum_{k\leq|\alpha|\leq N_{0}}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\sum_{k\leq|\alpha|\leq N_{0}}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}},

(1.22)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}^{k}_{N_{0}}(t)\sim\;

\displaystyle\sum_{k+1\leq|\alpha|\leq N_{0}}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{k\leq|\alpha|\leq N_{0}}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{{}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k+1\leq|\alpha|\leq N_{0}-1}\varepsilon^{2}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\sum_{k+2\leq|\alpha|\leq N_{0}-1}\varepsilon^{2}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}},

(1.23)

respectively, where
k=0,1
and the constant
N_{0}
will be determined later.

Our first main result gives the uniform global estimate with respect to
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
of solutions to the one-species VMB system (
1.2
).

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
. Introduce the following constants in sequence

\begin{split}&N_{0}\geq 3\;(N_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}),\quad N=\left[\frac{5}{3}N_{0}\right],\quad 1<s<3/2,\quad\vartheta=\frac{s-1}{2},\\
&l_{1}\geq 4N\quad\text{and}\;\quad l_{2}-l_{1}=\frac{N-1}{2}.\end{split}

(1.24)

If there exists a small constant
\delta_{1}>0
independent of
\varepsilon
such that the initial data

\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(0):=\mathcal{E}_{N}(0)+{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(0)+{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}_{N,l_{2}}(0)+\mathcal{E}_{-s}(0)\leq\delta_{1},

then the one-species VMB system (
1.2
) admits a unique global solution

(f^{\varepsilon},E^{\varepsilon},B^{\varepsilon})
satisfying

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{s}(1+t)^{\frac{s}{2}}\mathcal{E}_{N_{0}}^{0}(t)+\varepsilon^{1+s}(1+t)^{\frac{1+s}{2}}\mathcal{E}_{N_{0}}^{1}(t)\leq C\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(0),

(1.25)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{N}(t)+{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)+{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}_{N,l_{2}}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{-s}(t)\leq C\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(0)

(1.26)

for any
t\geq 0
and some positive constant
C>0
independent of
\varepsilon
.

The second main result is on the hydrodynamic limit from the one-species VMB system (
1.2
)
to the incompressible NSFM system.

Theorem 1.2
.

Let
(f^{\varepsilon},E^{\varepsilon},B^{\varepsilon})
be the
global solutions to the one-species VMB system (
1.2
) constructed in Theorem

1.1
with initial data
f_{0}^{\varepsilon}={f}_{0}^{\varepsilon}(x,v),E_{0}^{\varepsilon}=E_{0}^{\varepsilon}(x),B_{0}^{\varepsilon}=B_{0}^{\varepsilon}(x)
. Suppose that there exist scalar functions
(\rho_{0},\theta_{0})=(\rho_{0}(x),\theta_{0}(x))
and vector-valued functions
(u_{0},E_{0},B_{0})=(u_{0}(x),E_{0}(x),B_{0}(x))
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{f}_{0}^{\varepsilon}\to{f}_{0}\;\text{ strongly in }H^{N}_{x}L^{2}_{v},\;\;E_{0}^{\varepsilon}\to E_{0}\;\text{ and }\;B_{0}^{\varepsilon}\to B_{0}\;\text{ strongly in }H^{N}_{x}\end{split}

(1.27)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
and
{f}_{0}={f}_{0}(x,v)
is of the form

\displaystyle\begin{split}\!\!\!f_{0}=\;&\Big(\rho_{0}+u_{0}\cdot v+\theta_{0}\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\Big)\mu^{1/2}.\end{split}

(1.28)

Then there hold

\begin{split}&{f}^{\varepsilon}\to{f}\;\text{ weakly}\!-\!*\text{ in }L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}L^{2}_{v})\text{ and strongly in }C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}),\\
&E^{\varepsilon}\to E\;\text{ weakly}\!-\!*\text{ in }L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})\text{ and strongly in }C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{x}),\\
&B^{\varepsilon}\to B\;\text{ weakly}\!-\!*\text{ in }L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})\text{ and strongly in }C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{x})\end{split}

(1.29)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
and
f={f}(t,x,v)
has the form

\displaystyle\begin{split}f=\;&\Big(\rho+u\cdot v+\theta\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\Big){\mu}^{1/2}.\end{split}

(1.30)

Moreover, the above mass density
\rho=\rho(t,x)
, bulk velocity
u=u(t,x)
, temperature
\theta=\theta(t,x)
, electric field
E=E(t,x)
and magnetic field
B=B(t,x)
satisfy

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left(\rho,u,\theta,E,B\right)\in C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})\end{split}

(1.31)

and the following incompressible NSFM system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\partial_{t}u+u\cdot\nabla_{x}u-\nu\Delta_{x}u+\nabla_{x}P=E+u\times B,&\nabla_{x}\cdot u=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta+u\cdot\nabla_{x}\theta-\kappa\Delta_{x}\theta=0,&\rho+\theta=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}E-\nabla_{x}\times B=-u,&\nabla_{x}\cdot E=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}B+\nabla_{x}\times E=0,&\nabla_{x}\cdot B=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle u(0)=\mathcal{P}u_{0},\;\theta(0)=\frac{3}{5}\theta_{0}-\frac{2}{5}\rho_{0},\;E(0)=E_{0},B(0)=B_{0},&\end{array}\right.

(1.32)

where
\mathcal{P}
is the Leray projection, and the viscosity coefficient
\nu
and the heat conductivity coefficient
\kappa
are defined in (
4.33
)
and (
4.34
), respectively.

Remark 1.3
.

The results given in Theorems
1.1
and
1.2
indicate
that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq\infty}\Big\{\Big\|F^{\varepsilon}-\mu-\varepsilon\Big(\rho+u\cdot v+\theta\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\Big){\mu}\Big\|_{H^{N}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\Big\}=o(\varepsilon),

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq\infty}\Big\{\big\|E^{\varepsilon}-E\big\|_{H^{N-1}_{x}}+\big\|B^{\varepsilon}-B\big\|_{H^{N-1}_{x}}\Big\}=o(1),

which eventually justifies that the incompressible NSFM system (
1.32
), as the first order
approximation, is the hydrodynamic limit of the one-species VMB system (
1.2
).

Remark 1.4
.

It is worth pointing out the mechanism to introduce two different weight functions
\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(\alpha,\beta)
and
\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)
in (
1.14
) and (
1.17
). In fact,
the first weight function
\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(\alpha,\beta)
is used to overcome the velocity growth in the Lorentz force term, and is friendly to the
transport term
\frac{1}{\varepsilon}v\cdot\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}
in the sense that it does not involve any growth in velocity.
However, in this process the Lorentz force term brings a trouble term

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha^{\prime}|=1}\Big\langle-v\times\partial_{x}^{\alpha^{\prime}}B^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{I}\!-\!\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon},\;\overline{w}^{2}_{l_{1}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}\!-\!\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}},

(1.33)

which can not be controlled by the extra dissipation in (
1.16
) brought by the first weight, because
the critical term
\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}^{2}
can only reach a time decay rate
(1+t)^{-1}
, less than the required decay rate
(1+t)^{-(1+\vartheta)}
, cf. (
1.25
).
Thus, we have to design the second weight function
\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)
, which contributes another microscopic dissipation
in (
1.19
) and control the Lorentz force term (
1.33
) under the help of the interpolation inequality, cf.
Section
2
for more details.

Note that this usage of two different weight functions is fundamentally different from
[
12
,
29
]
,
wherein the time decay rate is fast enough and only velocity growth need such treatment.

Remark 1.5
.

It is noteworthy that the more challenging diffusive limit of the one-species VMB system with cutoff soft potentials still remains unsolved,
although diffusive limit of the two-species VMB system with cutoff soft potentials
-1\leq\gamma<0
has been justified by

[
29
]
. In fact, the approach, which we employ in this paper by applying two interactional weighted energy estimates, is not applicable to the one-species VMB system with cutoff soft potentials.
To illustrate this, let us define the second weight function

{w}_{l}(\alpha,\beta):=\langle v\rangle^{l-m|\alpha|-(m-\frac{\gamma}{2}-1)|\beta|}e^{\frac{q\langle v\rangle^{2}}{(1+t)^{\vartheta}}}

and take the
L^{2}_{x,v}
estimate of
{w}_{l}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}
. In this process,
the transport term and the Lorentz force term will bring two trouble terms

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\langle v\rangle{w}_{l}(\alpha+e_{i},\beta-e_{i})\partial_{\beta-e_{i}}^{\alpha+e_{i}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\end{split}

(1.34)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha^{\prime}|=1}\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}^{2}\|\langle v\rangle^{-\gamma}w_{l}(|\alpha|\!-\!1,|\beta|\!+\!1)\nabla_{v}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}\!}(\mathbf{I}\!-\!\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2},

(1.35)

respectively. On the one hand, to guarantee that (
1.34
) can be absorbed by the dissipation generated from
the
L^{2}_{x,v}
estimate of
{w}_{l}(\alpha+e_{i},\beta-e_{i})\partial_{\beta-e_{i}}^{\alpha+e_{i}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}
, we have to design the iteration with time decay factor
(1+t)^{-(1+\vartheta)}
for one more velocity derivative.
On the other hand,
the time decay rate of
\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}^{2}
in the one-species VMB system (
1.2
) can only reach

\varepsilon^{-{\frac{2s+5}{2}}}(1+t)^{-\frac{2s+5}{4}},

which is obviously much slower than the two-species VMB system
[
29
]
and has a singularity
\varepsilon^{-{\frac{2s+5}{2}}}
. Due to
this weak time decay rate, the term (
1.35
) is out of control. Consequently, diffusive limit of the one-species VMB system (
1.2
) with cutoff soft potentials still remains unsolved.

1.5.
Difficulties and Innovations

In this subsection, we outline the difficulties and innovations proposed in this paper.

Our analysis is based on the uniform weighted energy estimates with respect to
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
globally in time, which eventually leads to the incompressible NSFM limit.
To deal with the one-species VMB system (
1.2
) for the hard potentials
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
, we are faced with considerable difficulties induced by the weak dissipation
of the linearized Boltzmann operator and the slow time decay rate of the electromagnetic field.
In what follows, we point out the critical technical points in our treatment.

1.5.1.
Velocity weighted
H_{x,v}^{N}
energy estimate

Our velocity weighted
H_{x,v}^{N}
energy estimate contains some new ingredients.
The starting point is the following fundamental
H^{N}_{x}L^{2}_{v}
energy estimate for the VMB system (
1.2
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\mathcal{E}_{N}(t)+\mathcal{D}_{N}(t)\\
\lesssim\;&\underbrace{\mathcal{E}_{N}(t)\left(\|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{N-2}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}+\|\langle v\rangle(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{N-1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}\right)}_{:=D_{1}}\\
\;&+\!\underbrace{\varepsilon^{3}\|\nabla_{x}[E^{\varepsilon},\!B^{\varepsilon}]\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}\Big(\varepsilon\|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{x}^{N}\!(\mathbf{I}\!-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\!+\!\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{N-1}\!(\mathbf{I}\!-\!\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\Big)}_{:=D_{2}}+\cdots.\end{split}

(1.36)

The velocity growth of
D_{1}
and
D_{2}
in (
1.36
) urges us to design appropriate weighted energy estimate.

On the one hand, to handle the velocity growth of
D_{1}
in (
1.36
), we design a new time-velocity weight function
\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(\alpha,\beta)
defined in (
1.14
) and consider the corresponding weighted energy
{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)
defined in (
1.15
).
The advantage of this weight
\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(\alpha,\beta)
is that the transport term does
not involve any growth in velocity, namely,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&\Big\langle-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}\left[v\cdot\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right],w^{2}_{l_{1}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L_{x,v}^{2}}\\
=\;&\Big\langle-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\partial_{\beta-e_{i}}^{\alpha+e_{i}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon},w^{2}_{l_{1}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L_{x,v}^{2}}\\
\lesssim\;&\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(\alpha+e_{i},\beta-e_{i})\partial_{\beta-e_{i}}^{\alpha+e_{i}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}},\end{split}

where we have used the notation
e_{i}
to denote the multi-index with the
i
-th element unit and the rest zero, as well as the fact

\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(\alpha,\beta)=\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(\alpha+e_{i},\beta-e_{i}).

The usage of the weight
\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(\alpha,\beta)
will generate an extra dissipation term
(
1.16
), but this requires a certain time decay rate of
E^{\varepsilon}
and
B^{\varepsilon}
. However, when performing
the corresponding weighted energy estimate for Lorentz force terms, we find that for
|\alpha^{\prime}|=1
,

\displaystyle\Big\langle-v\times\partial_{x}^{\alpha^{\prime}}B^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{I}\!-\!\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon},w^{2}_{l_{1}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}\!-\!\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\underbrace{\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}^{2}\|\langle v\rangle\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(|\alpha|\!-\!1,|\beta|\!+\!1)\nabla_{v}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}\!}(\mathbf{I}\!-\!\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}}_{:=D_{3}}+\frac{\eta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}\!-\!\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}.

Here
D_{3}
can not be controlled by the extra dissipation given in (
1.16
), in that the time decay rate of
\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}^{2}
can only reach
(1+t)^{-1}
, less than the required decay rate
(1+t)^{-(1+\vartheta)}
, cf. (
2.1
). To overcome this difficulty,
our treatment is to utilize the interpolation method and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality with small
\eta
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}D_{3}\lesssim\;&\Big[\left(\varepsilon^{2-\varrho}\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)^{\frac{2}{\varrho}}\|\langle v\rangle^{\widetilde{\ell}}\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(|\alpha|-1,|\beta|+1)\nabla_{v}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\Big]^{\varrho}\\
\;&\times\Big[\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(|\alpha|-1,|\beta|+1)\nabla_{v}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\Big]^{1-\varrho}\\
\lesssim\;&\left(\varepsilon^{2-\varrho}\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)^{\frac{2}{\varrho}}\|\underbrace{\langle v\rangle^{\widetilde{\ell}}\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(|\alpha|-1,|\beta|+1)}\nabla_{v}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\\
\;&+\frac{\eta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(|\alpha|-1,|\beta|+1)\nabla_{v}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\\
\lesssim\;&\left(\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)^{2\widetilde{\ell}}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\overline{w}_{l_{2}}(|\alpha|-1,|\beta|+1)\nabla_{v}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\cdots,\end{split}

(1.37)

where
|\alpha^{\prime}|=1
,
l_{2}=\widetilde{\ell}+l_{1}
and
\varrho={\widetilde{\ell}}^{-1}
.
This indicates that once the magnetic field
\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

has some decay, the total decay of
(\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})^{2\widetilde{\ell}}
will be large enough
as the weight index
\widetilde{\ell}
increases.
Therefore, motivated by
[
12
,
29
]
, we design the second weight
\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)
defined in (
1.17
) to control
the underbraced term in (
1.37
).

The advantage of the weight
\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)
is that it can balance the singularity and velocity growth
in the weighted energy estimate. More precisely, for the
L^{2}_{x,v}
-estimate of

\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}
with
|\alpha|+|\beta|=n\leq N-1
, the most trouble term induced by the Lorentz field can be controlled by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&\sum_{|\alpha^{\prime}|=1}\Big\langle-v\times\partial_{x}^{\alpha^{\prime}}B^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon},\widetilde{w}^{2}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\\
\lesssim\;&\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}^{2}\sum_{|\alpha^{\prime}|=1}\|\langle v\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(|\alpha|-1,|\beta|+1)\nabla_{v}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\\
\;&+\frac{\eta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\\
\lesssim\;&\varepsilon^{3}\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}^{2}{(1+t)^{\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}}}{(1+t)^{-\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}}}\!\!\sum_{|\alpha^{\prime}|=1}\!\!\|\langle v\rangle\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(|\alpha|\!-\!1,|\beta|\!+\!1)\nabla_{v}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha\!-\!\alpha^{\prime}}\!(\mathbf{I}\!-\!\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\\
\;&\times\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(|\alpha|-1,|\beta|+1)\nabla_{v}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\cdots,\end{split}

(1.38)

where we have used the fact

\langle v\rangle\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)=\langle v\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha-e_{i},\beta+e_{i}).

However, the shortcoming of this weight
\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)
lies in that the transport term involves velocity growth, that is,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&\Big\langle-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}\left[v\cdot\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right],\widetilde{w}^{2}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L_{x,v}^{2}}\\
\lesssim\;&C_{\eta}\|\langle v\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha+e_{i},\beta-e_{i})\partial_{\beta-e_{i}}^{\alpha+e_{i}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}.\end{split}

(1.39)

In order to absorb the first term on the right-hand side of (
1.39
) by the microscopic dissipation induced by
the
L^{2}_{x,v}
-estimate of
\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha+e_{i},\beta-e_{i})\partial_{\beta-e_{i}}^{\alpha+e_{i}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}
, it is essential for us to arrange different time increments for the various space-velocity derivatives of
f^{\varepsilon}
as

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq N-1}(1+t)^{-|\beta|\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}}\left\|\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}.

Consequently, with this arrangement, the first term on the right-hand side of (
1.38
) can be controlled, provided that

\varepsilon^{3}\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}^{2}{(1+t)^{1+\vartheta}}\lesssim C,

cf. the proof of Proposition
2.7
for more details.

On the other hand, the trouble term
D_{2}
in (
1.36
) is associated with the
N
-th order space-velocity derivative of
(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}
. Observing that
D_{2}
includes the factor
\varepsilon^{3}\|\nabla_{x}[E^{\varepsilon},\!B^{\varepsilon}]\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}
, which possess the time decay rate
(1+t)^{-(1+\vartheta)}
, we therefore attempt to consider weighted energy estimate of
\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}
with
N
-th order spatial-velocity derivatives
|\alpha|+|\beta|=N.

However,
the usage of the weight function
\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,0)
also generates a severe singularity when we handle the
N
-th order space derivative on the linear Boltzmann operator
L
, that is

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\langle L\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{\varepsilon},\widetilde{w}^{2}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,0)\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\geq\;

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma_{0}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\|\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,0)\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}-\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}},\quad|\alpha|=N,

where the last term includes the singular macroscopic quantity
\dfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}
and is out of control.
We solve this difficulty by first multiplying the first equation in (
1.2
) with
\varepsilon
and then making
\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}^{2}(\alpha,0)
-weighted energy estimate
(|\alpha|=N)
, namely

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\left\|\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,0)\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\langle L\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{\varepsilon},\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}^{2}(\alpha,0)\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\left\|\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,0)\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\langle L\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon},\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}^{2}(\alpha,0)\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\langle L\partial^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon},\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}^{2}(\alpha,0)\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\;

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\left\|\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,0)\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{0}}{\varepsilon}\left\|\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,0)\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}^{2}-\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle-\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2},\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad

then the last two terms on the right-hand side can be controlled by the dissipation
\mathcal{D}_{N}(t)
.

Meanwhile, due to the loss of the highest order derivatives of electric field
\varepsilon\|\nabla^{N}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}
in the dissipation (
1.13
), observing that

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|=N}\Big\langle\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(E^{\varepsilon}\cdot v\sqrt{\mu}),\varepsilon\widetilde{w}^{2}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,0)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L_{x,v}^{2}}\lesssim\varepsilon\|\nabla^{N}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\nabla^{N}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}},

we use a time factor
(1+t)^{-\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}}
to calculate it.
That is, the desired
L^{2}_{x,v}
energy estimate of the
N
-th order space derivative is
(1+t)^{-\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}}\varepsilon\|\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,0)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}
, which implies

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|=N}\Big\langle\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(E^{\varepsilon}\cdot v\sqrt{\mu}),(1+t)^{-\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}}\varepsilon\widetilde{w}^{2}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,0)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L_{x,v}^{2}}\lesssim\;\eta\varepsilon^{2}(1+t)^{-(1+\vartheta)}\|\nabla^{N}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\mathcal{D}_{N}(t).

In conclusion, we eventually
design the following velocity weighted
N
-th order energy

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|+|\beta|=N,\\
|\alpha|\neq N\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\!(1+t)^{-(|\beta|+1)\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}}\left\|\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+(1+t)^{-\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}}\varepsilon\sum_{|\alpha|=N}\!\!\!\left\|\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,0)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}.

For more details, please see the proof of Proposition
2.6
.

1.5.2.
Time decay estimates of lower-order derivatives
\varepsilon^{s}\mathcal{E}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t)
and
\varepsilon^{1+s}\mathcal{E}^{1}_{N_{0}}(t)

To close the a priori estimates, we also need sufficient time decay rates of

\varepsilon^{s}\mathcal{E}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t)
and
\varepsilon^{1+s}\mathcal{E}^{1}_{N_{0}}(t)
. Due to the slow time decay rate of the linearized VMB system
[
11
]
and the singularity
1/\varepsilon
in front of the nonlinear terms, the method of combining the semigroup theory of linearized equation with the Duhamel principle is no longer applicable to the nonlinear problem. To overcome this difficulty, inspired by
[
26
]
, we employ the interpolation and energy estimate in negative Sobolev space
\|\Lambda^{-s}\big(f^{\varepsilon},E^{\varepsilon},B^{\varepsilon}\big)\|
to obtain the time decay rate.
In contrast to the two-species VMB system
[
29
]
, one
major difference lies in the Lorentz force term

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\left[(v\times B^{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla_{v}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right],\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\neq 0

(1.40)

when
|\alpha|>0
. Therefore, in the energy estimate of
\mathcal{E}^{1}_{N_{0}}(t)
, the tricky term (
1.40
) is
handled by

\displaystyle\eqref{decay-kunnan-1}\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\!\Big[\sum_{1\leq|\alpha^{\prime}|\leq N_{0}-1}\|\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{3}_{x}}\|\partial^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\!\|_{L^{6}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\!\|\nabla^{N_{0}}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\Big]\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{N_{0}-1}_{x}}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t),

which further implies that we can only obtain
the energy inequality

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\mathcal{E}^{1}_{N_{0}}(t)+\mathcal{D}^{1}_{N_{0}}(t)\lesssim\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{N_{0}-1}_{x}}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t).

(1.41)

The last nonhomogeneous source term in (
1.41
) hinders us from applying the interpolation and
negative Sobolev space mentioned above.
Fortunately, thanks to (
3.1
) and (
3.10
), we find that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\left\{\varepsilon^{s}(1+t)^{\frac{s}{2}}\mathcal{E}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t)\right\}+(1+t)^{-p}\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon^{s}(1+\tau)^{\frac{s}{2}+p}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\\
\lesssim\;&{\mathcal{E}}_{N}(0)+\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\left\{{\mathcal{E}}_{N_{0}+\frac{s}{2}}(\tau)+{\mathcal{E}}_{-s}(\tau)\right\},\end{split}

indicating that once
\varepsilon(1+t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{N_{0}-1}_{x}}\lesssim\eta
is small enough, the last
nonhomogeneous term in (
1.41
) will be controlled in the way

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&\varepsilon^{1+s}\int_{0}^{t}(1+\tau)^{\frac{1+s}{2}+p}\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{H^{N_{0}-1}_{x}}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\lesssim\eta\varepsilon^{s}\int_{0}^{t}(1+\tau)^{\frac{s}{2}+p}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\end{split}

in the process of obtaining the time decay rate
(1+t)^{\frac{1+s}{2}}
of
\varepsilon^{1+s}\mathcal{E}^{1}_{N_{0}}(t)
from (
1.41
).
Consequently, based on the analysis above, we are able to obtain the time decay estimates of
\varepsilon^{s}\mathcal{E}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t)
and
\varepsilon^{1+s}\mathcal{E}^{1}_{N_{0}}(t)
, please see the proof of Proposition
3.1
for details.
By combining the above strategies, we eventually close the global a priori estimates successfully.

The rest of this paper is organized as follows. In Section
2
, we establish the uniform a priori weighted energy estimate. In Section
3
, we first obtain the time decay rate and close the a priori estimates, and then we give the proof of Theorem
1.1
. In section
4
, based on the uniform energy estimate with respect to
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
globally in time, we justify
the limit of the one-species VMB system (
1.2
) to the incompressible NSFM system (
1.32
), that is, give the proof of Theorem
1.2
.

2.
The a Priori Estimates

In this section, we deduce the uniform a priori estimates of the one-species VMB system (
1.2
) with respect to
\varepsilon\in(0,1]

globally in time.
For this purpose, we define the following time-weighted energy norm
X(t)
by

\displaystyle\begin{split}X(t):=\;&\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\Big\{\mathcal{E}_{N}(\tau)+{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(\tau)+{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}_{N,l_{2}}(\tau)+\mathcal{E}_{-s}(\tau)+\varepsilon^{s}(1+\tau)^{\frac{s}{2}}\mathcal{E}_{N_{0}}^{0}(\tau)\Big\}\\
\;&+\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\left\{\varepsilon^{1+s}(1+\tau)^{\frac{1+s}{2}}\mathcal{E}_{N_{0}}^{1}(\tau)+\varepsilon^{3}(1+\tau)^{{1+\vartheta}}\left\|\nabla_{x}\left[E^{\varepsilon}(\tau),B^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right]\right\|_{H^{2}_{x}}^{2}\right\}.\end{split}

(2.1)

Here, all the involved parameters are fixed to satisfy (
1.24
).

The construction of the weighted instant energy functionals
\mathcal{E}_{N}(t)
,
{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)
,

{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}_{N,l_{2}}(t)
and

\mathcal{E}_{-s}(t)
will be given in the following. Suppose that the one-species VMB system (
1.2
) admits a smooth solution
\left(f^{\varepsilon},E^{\varepsilon},B^{\varepsilon}\right)
over
0\leq t\leq T
for
0<T\leq\infty
, and the solution

\left(f^{\varepsilon},E^{\varepsilon},B^{\varepsilon}\right)
also satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}X(t)\leq\delta_{0},

(2.2)

where
\delta_{0}
is a suitable small positive constant to be determined later.

2.1.
Fundamental Energy Estimate

In this subsection, we aim to establish the uniform energy estimate without weight for the VMB system (
1.2
).

To be precise, we begin with decomposing the perturbation
f^{\varepsilon}
into

f^{\varepsilon}=\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}+(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon},

(2.3)

where the macro part
\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}=\Big[a^{\varepsilon}(t,x)+b^{\varepsilon}(t,x)\cdot v+c^{\varepsilon}(t,x)\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\Big]{\mu}^{1/2}

(2.4)

with
a^{\varepsilon}(t,x),b^{\varepsilon}(t,x),c^{\varepsilon}(t,x)
defined as

\displaystyle a^{\varepsilon}(t,x):=\langle\mu^{1/2},\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}},\quad b^{\varepsilon}(t,x):=\langle v\mu^{1/2},\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}},\quad c^{\varepsilon}(t,x):=\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\big\langle(|v|^{2}-3)\mu^{1/2},\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}.

We are now in a position to state the main result of this subsection.

Proposition 2.1
.

Let
N,l_{1},l_{2}
be fixed parameters stated in Theorem
1.1
.
Assume that
(f^{\varepsilon},E^{\varepsilon},B^{\varepsilon})
is a solution to the VMB system (
1.2
) defined on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}

and the
a priori
assumption (
2.2
) holds true for
\delta_{0}
small enough.
Then, there exists an energy functional
\mathcal{E}_{N}(t)
and the
corresponding energy dissipation functional
\mathcal{D}_{N}(t)
which satisfy (
2.28
), (
1.13
) respectively
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\mathcal{E}_{N}(t)+\mathcal{D}_{N}(t)\lesssim\;&\delta_{0}{\overline{\mathcal{D}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)+\delta_{0}{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}}_{N,l_{2}}(t).\end{split}

(2.5)

The main idea of proving Proposition

2.1
is that by combining direct energy methods
on the perturbation VMB system (
1.2
) and
the so-called macro-micro decomposition method to acquire the missing macro dissipations.

First of all, we find
a dissipation structure of the macroscopic part
\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}
.

Lemma 2.2
.

Let
\left(f^{\varepsilon},E^{\varepsilon},B^{\varepsilon}\right)
be the solution to the VMB system (
1.2
) defined on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
.
Then there exists an interactive functional
\mathcal{E}^{N}_{int}(t)
defined in (
2.18
) satisfying

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|\mathcal{E}^{N}_{int}(t)\right|\lesssim\;&\sum_{|\alpha|\leq N}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq N}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq N}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}},\end{split}

such that for any
t\geq 0
, there holds

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varepsilon\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\mathcal{E}^{N}_{int}(t)\!+\!\sum_{1\leq|\alpha|\leq N}\!\!\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\varepsilon^{2}\sum_{1\leq|\alpha|\leq N-1}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\varepsilon^{2}\sum_{2\leq|\alpha|\leq N-1}\!\!\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim\;&\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq N}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\mathcal{E}_{N}(t)\mathcal{D}_{N}(t).\end{split}

(2.6)

Proof.

We prove this lemma by an analysis of the macroscopic equations and the local conservation laws. Firstly,
plugging (
2.3
) and (
2.4
) into the first equation in (
1.2
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\partial_{t}\Big(a^{\varepsilon}+b^{\varepsilon}\cdot v+c^{\varepsilon}\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\Big)\sqrt{\mu}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\mu}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\Big(a^{\varepsilon}+b^{\varepsilon}\cdot v+c^{\varepsilon}\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\Big)-\sqrt{\mu}v\cdot E^{\varepsilon}\\
=&-\partial_{t}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}+g^{\varepsilon}(f^{\varepsilon},E^{\varepsilon},B^{\varepsilon})\end{split}

with
g^{\varepsilon}(f^{\varepsilon},E^{\varepsilon},B^{\varepsilon})
given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}g^{\varepsilon}:=-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}v\cdot\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}-\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}Lf^{\varepsilon}-\frac{\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}(\sqrt{\mu}f^{\varepsilon})}{\sqrt{\mu}}-v\times B^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Gamma(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}).\end{split}

(2.7)

Fixing
(t,x)
and comparing the coefficients on both sides,
we thereby obtain the so-called macroscopic equations

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sqrt{\mu}:&\qquad\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Big(a^{\varepsilon}-\frac{3}{2}c^{\varepsilon}\Big)=\mathcal{G}_{1},\\[5.69054pt]
v_{i}\sqrt{\mu}:&\qquad\displaystyle\partial_{t}b_{i}^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\partial_{i}\Big(a^{\varepsilon}-\frac{3}{2}c^{\varepsilon}\Big)-E^{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{G}_{2}^{i},\\[5.69054pt]
v_{i}^{2}\sqrt{\mu}:&\qquad\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}c^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\partial_{i}b_{i}^{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{G}_{3}^{i},\\[5.69054pt]
v_{i}v_{j}\sqrt{\mu}(i\neq j):&\qquad\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(\partial_{i}b_{j}^{\varepsilon}+\partial_{j}b_{i}^{\varepsilon}\right)=\mathcal{G}_{4}^{i,j},\\[5.69054pt]
v_{i}|v|^{2}\sqrt{\mu}:&\qquad\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\partial_{i}c^{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{G}_{5}^{i}.\end{array}\right.

(2.8)

Here the right-hand terms
\mathcal{G}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{2}^{i},\mathcal{G}_{3}^{i},\mathcal{G}_{4}^{i,j}
and
\mathcal{G}_{5}^{i}(i,j=1,2,3)
are all of the form

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\langle-\partial_{t}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}+g^{\varepsilon}(f^{\varepsilon},E^{\varepsilon},B^{\varepsilon}),\zeta\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}},\end{split}

where
\zeta
is a (different) linear combination of the basis
\left[\sqrt{\mu},\,v_{i}\sqrt{\mu},\,v_{i}v_{j}\sqrt{\mu},\,v_{i}|v|^{2}\sqrt{\mu}\right]
.

The second set of equations we consider are the local conservation laws satisfied
by
(a^{\varepsilon}\!,\!b^{\varepsilon}\!,\!c^{\varepsilon})
. To derive them, multiplying the first equation in (
1.2
) by the collision
invariant
(1,v,\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2})\sqrt{\mu}
and integrating the resulting identity over
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, we derive by
using (
2.4
) that
a^{\varepsilon}
,
b^{\varepsilon}
and
c^{\varepsilon}
obey the local macroscopic balance laws of mass, moment and energy

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\partial_{t}a^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot b^{\varepsilon}=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}b^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}(a^{\varepsilon}+c^{\varepsilon})-E^{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{X}_{b^{\varepsilon}},\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}c^{\varepsilon}+\frac{2}{3\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot b^{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{X}_{c^{\varepsilon}}.\end{array}\right.

(2.9)

Here
\mathcal{X}_{b^{\varepsilon}}
and
\mathcal{X}_{c^{\varepsilon}}
are given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{X}_{b^{\varepsilon}}&:=\varepsilon a^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}+b^{\varepsilon}\times B^{\varepsilon}-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v\cdot\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}v\sqrt{\mu}{\mathrm{d}}v,\\
\mathcal{X}_{c^{\varepsilon}}&:=\frac{2}{3}\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\cdot b^{\varepsilon}-\frac{1}{3\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v\cdot\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}|v|^{2}\sqrt{\mu}{\mathrm{d}}v.\end{split}

(2.10)

Next, applying the analogous arguments employed in
[
20
]
, which is based on the analysis of the macro fluid-type system (
2.8
) and the local conservation
laws (
2.9
),
we can obtain that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varepsilon\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\mathcal{E}^{(\alpha)}_{a^{\varepsilon},b^{\varepsilon},c^{\varepsilon}}(t)+\left\|\nabla_{x}(a^{\varepsilon},b^{\varepsilon},c^{\varepsilon})\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim\;&\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\|\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\left\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\left\|\nabla_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\;&+\varepsilon^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\sum_{j\neq i}\left\|\big\langle(\mathcal{G}_{3}^{i}+\mathcal{G}_{3}^{j}+\mathcal{G}_{4}^{i,j}+\mathcal{G}_{5}^{i}+\mathcal{X}_{b^{\varepsilon}}),\zeta\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\quad\text{for}\quad|\alpha|=0,\end{split}

(2.11)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varepsilon\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\mathcal{E}^{(\alpha)}_{a^{\varepsilon},b^{\varepsilon},c^{\varepsilon}}(t)+\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}(a^{\varepsilon},b^{\varepsilon},c^{\varepsilon})\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim\;&\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\;&+\varepsilon^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\sum_{j\neq i}\left\|\big\langle\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\mathcal{G}_{3}^{i}+\mathcal{G}_{3}^{j}+\mathcal{G}_{4}^{i,j}+\mathcal{G}_{5}^{i}+\mathcal{X}_{b^{\varepsilon}}),\zeta\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\quad\text{ for}\quad 1\leq|\alpha|\leq N-1.\end{split}

(2.12)

The details are omitted for
simplicity. Here, we only point out the representation of

\mathcal{E}^{\mathbf{(\alpha)}}_{a^{\varepsilon},b^{\varepsilon},c^{\varepsilon}}(t)
as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{E}^{\mathbf{(\alpha)}}_{a^{\varepsilon},b^{\varepsilon},c^{\varepsilon}}(t)=\;&\kappa_{1}\big\langle-\nabla_{x}\cdot\partial_{x}^{\alpha}b,\,\partial_{x}^{\alpha}a\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x}}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\big\langle\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon},\,\chi_{a^{\varepsilon}}^{i}\partial_{i}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}a^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\\
\;&+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\sum_{j\neq i}\big\langle\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon},\,\chi_{b^{\varepsilon}}^{i,j}\partial_{j}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}b_{i}^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\big\langle\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon},\,\chi_{b^{\varepsilon}}^{i}\partial_{i}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}b_{i}^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\\
\;&+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\big\langle\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon},\,\chi_{c^{\varepsilon}}^{i}\partial_{i}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}c^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\quad\text{ for}\quad 0\leq|\alpha|\leq N-1,\end{split}

where
\chi_{a^{\varepsilon}}^{i}
,
\chi_{b^{\varepsilon}}^{i,j}
,
\chi_{b^{\varepsilon}}^{i}
and
\chi_{c^{\varepsilon}}^{i}
are dissimilar linear combinations of

\left[\sqrt{\mu},v_{i}\sqrt{\mu},v_{i}v_{j}\sqrt{\mu},v_{i}|v|^{2}\sqrt{\mu}\right]

and
\kappa_{1}>0
is a small constant.

To estimate
E^{\varepsilon}
, using the Maxwell system in (
1.2
), (
2.9
) and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality with
\eta
, one can deduce that, for any
1\leq|\alpha|\leq N-1
, there holds

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}=\varepsilon^{2}\big\langle\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E^{\varepsilon},\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}\\
=\;&\varepsilon^{2}\Big\langle\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E^{\varepsilon},\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\Big[\partial_{t}b^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}(a^{\varepsilon}+c^{\varepsilon})-\mathcal{X}_{b^{\varepsilon}}\Big]\Big\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}\\
=\;&\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\!\varepsilon^{2}\big\langle\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E^{\varepsilon},\partial^{\alpha}_{x}b^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}\!-\!\varepsilon^{2}\big\langle\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon},\partial^{\alpha}_{x}b^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}+\eta\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}(a^{\varepsilon}+c^{\varepsilon})\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}\\
\;&+C_{\eta}\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\mathcal{X}_{b^{\varepsilon}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}.\end{split}

(2.13)

Further, for the second term on the right-hand side of (
2.13
), applying (
1.2
)
2
gives rise to

\displaystyle\begin{split}\varepsilon^{2}\big\langle\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon},\partial^{\alpha}_{x}b^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}&=\varepsilon^{2}\big\langle\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\nabla_{x}\times B^{\varepsilon}-b^{\varepsilon}),\partial^{\alpha}_{x}b^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}\\
&=\varepsilon^{2}\big\langle\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\times B^{\varepsilon},\partial^{\alpha}_{x}b^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}-\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}b^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}\\
&=\varepsilon^{2}\big\langle\partial^{\alpha}_{x}B^{\varepsilon},\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\times b^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}-\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}b^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}.\end{split}

(2.14)

Hence, putting (
2.14
) into (
2.13
) and choosing
\eta
small enough, we find that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varepsilon\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}{\mathcal{E}}_{E^{\varepsilon}}^{(\alpha)}(t)+\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}\\
\lesssim\;&\varepsilon^{4}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\times B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\mathcal{X}_{b^{\varepsilon}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}\quad\text{for}\quad|\alpha|=1,\end{split}

(2.15)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\varepsilon\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}{\mathcal{E}}_{E^{\varepsilon}}^{(\alpha)}(t)+\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\;&\varepsilon^{4}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\mathcal{X}_{b^{\varepsilon}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}\end{split}

(2.16)

for
2\leq|\alpha|\leq N-1
, where
{\mathcal{E}}_{E^{\varepsilon}}^{(\alpha)}(t)
is defined as

{\mathcal{E}}_{E^{\varepsilon}}^{(\alpha)}(t):=-\varepsilon\big\langle\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E^{\varepsilon},\partial^{\alpha}_{x}b^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}\quad\text{ for}\quad 1\leq|\alpha|\leq N-1.

To obtain the dissipation of
B^{\varepsilon}
, by using the Maxwell system in (
1.2
) and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality with
\eta
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\times B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}\\
=\;&\varepsilon^{2}\big\langle\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\times B^{\varepsilon},\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}+b^{\varepsilon})\big\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}\\
=\;&\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\varepsilon^{2}\big\langle\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\times B^{\varepsilon},\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}-\varepsilon^{2}\big\langle\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\times\partial_{t}B^{\varepsilon},\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}+\varepsilon^{2}\big\langle\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\times B^{\varepsilon}~,~\partial^{\alpha}_{x}b^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}\\
\leq\;&\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\varepsilon^{2}\big\langle\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\times B^{\varepsilon},\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial^{{\alpha}}_{x}\nabla_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}+\eta\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\times B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}+C_{\eta}\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}b^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}.\end{split}

Thereby, we derive that for any
1\leq|\alpha|\leq N-2
, there holds

\begin{split}&\varepsilon\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}{\mathcal{E}}_{B^{\varepsilon}}^{(\alpha)}(t)+\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\times B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\times E^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}b^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2},\end{split}

(2.17)

where
{\mathcal{E}}_{B^{\varepsilon}}^{(\alpha)}(t)
is defined as

{\mathcal{E}}_{B^{\varepsilon}}^{(\alpha)}(t):=-\varepsilon\big\langle\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\times B^{\varepsilon},\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle_{L_{x}^{2}}\quad\text{ for}\quad 0\leq|\alpha|\leq N-2.

Finally, set

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{N}_{int}(t):=\sum_{0\leq|\alpha|\leq N-1}\mathcal{E}^{(\alpha)}_{a^{\varepsilon},b^{\varepsilon},c^{\varepsilon}}(t)+\kappa_{2}\sum_{1\leq|\alpha|\leq N-1}\mathcal{E}^{(\alpha)}_{E^{\varepsilon}}(t)+\kappa_{3}\sum_{1\leq|\alpha|\leq N-2}{\mathcal{E}}_{B^{\varepsilon}}^{(\alpha)}(t)

(2.18)

with
0<\kappa_{3}<\kappa_{2}\ll 1
.
It follows from (
2.11
), (
2.12
),
(
2.15
), (
2.16
) and (
2.17
) that

\displaystyle\varepsilon\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\mathcal{E}^{N}_{int}(t)+\!\!\!\sum_{0\leq|\alpha|\leq N-1}\!\!\!\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\varepsilon^{2}\!\!\sum_{1\leq|\alpha|\leq N-1}\!\!\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\varepsilon^{2}\!\!\sum_{2\leq|\alpha|\leq N-1}\!\!\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{0\leq|\alpha|\leq N}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\sum_{0\leq|\alpha|\leq N-1}\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\mathcal{X}_{b^{\varepsilon}}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}}^{2}

(2.19)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}\!\!\sum_{0\leq|\alpha|\leq N-1}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\sum_{j\neq i}\left\|\big\langle\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\mathcal{G}_{3}^{i}+\mathcal{G}_{3}^{j}+\mathcal{G}_{4}^{i,j}+\mathcal{G}_{5}^{i}),\,\zeta\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}.

By referring back to (
2.7
), (
2.10
) for
g^{\varepsilon}
and
\mathcal{X}_{b^{\varepsilon}}
, and employing (
A.3
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{0\leq|\alpha|\leq N-1}\!\!\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\mathcal{X}_{b^{\varepsilon}}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\sum_{0\leq|\alpha|\leq N-1}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\sum_{j\neq i}\left\|\big\langle\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\mathcal{G}_{3}^{i}+\mathcal{G}_{3}^{j}+\mathcal{G}_{4}^{i,j}+\mathcal{G}_{5}^{i}),\,\zeta\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim\;&\sum_{|\alpha|\leq N-1}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq N-1}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\mathcal{E}_{N}(t)\mathcal{D}_{N}(t).\end{split}

(2.20)

Hence, gathering (
2.19
) and (
2.20
) leads to the desired result (
2.6
).
This completes the proof of Lemma
2.2
.
∎

Next, we perform the following

H^{N}_{x}L^{2}_{v}
energy estimate for the VMB system (
1.2
).

Lemma 2.3
.

Let
(f^{\varepsilon},E^{\varepsilon},B^{\varepsilon})
be the solution of the VMB system (
1.2
) defined on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
and the
a priori
assumption (
2.2
) hold true for
\delta_{0}
small enough. Then, there holds

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\left[\left\|f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{H^{N}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\left\|(E^{\varepsilon},\!B^{\varepsilon})\right\|^{2}_{H^{N}_{x}}\right]\!+\!\frac{\sigma_{0}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\|(\mathbf{I}\!-\!\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{H^{N}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}+\varepsilon\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|b^{\varepsilon}|^{2}(a^{\varepsilon}+c^{\varepsilon})\mathrm{d}x\\
\lesssim\;&\delta_{0}\mathcal{D}_{N}(t)+\delta_{0}\left(\left\|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{H^{N-2}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}+\left\|\langle v\rangle(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{H^{N-1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}\right)\\
\;&+\frac{\delta_{0}}{(1+t)^{1+\vartheta}}\Big(\varepsilon\left\|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{x}^{N}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{N-1}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\Big).\end{split}

(2.21)

Proof.

The proof of (
2.21
) is divided into
two steps as follows.

Step 1.
L_{x,v}^{2}
-estimate of
f^{\varepsilon}
.

Taking the
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v})
inner product of the first equation of (
1.2
) with
f^{\varepsilon}
and then employing (
1.11
), we derive

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\left\|f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\underbrace{\Big\langle-E^{\varepsilon}\cdot v\mu^{1/2},f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}_{\mathcal{X}_{1}}+\frac{\sigma_{0}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\\
\lesssim\;&\underbrace{\Big\langle\frac{\varepsilon}{2}(v\cdot E^{\varepsilon}f^{\varepsilon}),f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}_{\mathcal{X}_{2}}+\underbrace{\Big\langle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Gamma(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}),f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}_{\mathcal{X}_{3}}.\end{split}

(2.22)

For the term
{\mathcal{X}_{1}}
, recalling the second and third equation of (
1.2
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{1}=\Big\langle E^{\varepsilon},\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}-\nabla_{x}\times B^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x}}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\left(\left\|E^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\left\|B^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\right).

By the macro-micro decomposition (
2.3
), we derive

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{X}_{2}=\;&\underbrace{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\langle v\cdot E^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon},\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}_{\mathcal{X}_{2,1}}+\underbrace{\varepsilon\langle v\cdot E^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon},(\mathbf{I}\!-\!\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}_{\mathcal{X}_{2,2}}+\underbrace{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\langle v\cdot E^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{I}\!-\!\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon},(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}_{\mathcal{X}_{2,3}}.\end{split}

Then we derive from (
2.4
), (
2.9
), (
2.10
) and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{X}_{2,1}=\;&-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}v\cdot E^{\varepsilon}\Big[\Big(a^{\varepsilon}+b^{\varepsilon}\cdot v+c^{\varepsilon}\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\Big)\sqrt{\mu}\Big]^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}v\\
=\;&-\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(a^{\varepsilon}+c^{\varepsilon})b^{\varepsilon}\cdot E^{\varepsilon}{\mathrm{d}}x\\
