\lesssim\;&\delta_{0}\sum_{n\leq N-1}(1+t)^{\sigma_{n,|\beta|-1}}\mathcal{D}_{l_{2}}^{n,|\beta|-1}(t)+\eta(1+t)^{\sigma_{N,|\beta|}}\mathcal{D}_{l_{2}}^{N,|\beta|}(t).\end{split}

Thus, the above estimates of
\mathcal{J}_{8,i}(i=1,2,3,4)
give us that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{J}_{8}\lesssim\;&\delta_{0}(1+t)^{\sigma_{N,|\beta|}}\mathcal{D}_{l_{2}}^{N,|\beta|+1}(t)+\delta_{0}\sum_{n\leq N-1}(1+t)^{\sigma_{n,|\beta|+1}}\mathcal{D}_{l_{2}}^{n,|\beta|+1}(t)+\eta(1+t)^{\sigma_{N,|\beta|}}\mathcal{D}_{l_{2}}^{N,|\beta|}(t)\\
\;&+\delta_{0}\sum_{n\leq N}(1+t)^{\sigma_{n,|\beta|}}\mathcal{D}_{l_{2}}^{n,|\beta|}(t)+\delta_{0}\sum_{n\leq N-1}(1+t)^{\sigma_{n,|\beta|-1}}\mathcal{D}_{l_{2}}^{n,|\beta|-1}(t),\end{split}

From (
A.4
) and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality with
\eta
, we deduce

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{J}_{9}\lesssim{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)\sum_{n\leq N}(1+t)^{\sigma_{n,|\beta|}}\mathcal{D}_{l_{2}}^{n,|\beta|}(t)+\eta(1+t)^{\sigma_{N,|\beta|}}\mathcal{D}_{l_{2}}^{N,|\beta|}(t).\end{split}

Taking the analogous argument of the term
\mathcal{Y}_{5}
in Proposition
2.4
,
one can easily find that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{J}_{10}\lesssim\mathcal{D}_{N}(t)+\varepsilon\mathcal{E}_{N}(t)\mathcal{D}_{N}(t)\lesssim\mathcal{D}_{N}(t)+\delta_{0}\mathcal{D}_{N}(t).\end{split}

As a consequence, with the aid of (
2.2
) and substituting all the above estimates of
\mathcal{J}_{6}\sim\mathcal{J}_{10}

into (
2.42
) and then choosing
\eta
small enough yield the desired estimate (
2.41
).

Consequently,
taking (
2.41
) the summation over
\left\{|\beta|=m,|\alpha|+|\beta|=N\right\}
for each given
1\leq m\leq N
, and then taking combination of those
N
estimates with properly chosen constant
C_{m}>0

(1\leq m\leq N)
, and then adding the resulting inequality into (
2.39
),
we conclude (
2.38
). This completes the proof of Lemma
2.6
.
∎

Next, we provide the lower-order weighted energy estimate with the weight
\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)
for
|\alpha|+|\beta|=n\leq N-1
as follows.

Lemma 2.7
.

Assume that the
a priori
assumption (
2.2
) holds true for
\delta_{0}
small enough.
Then, there holds

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{n\leq N-1}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{l_{2}}^{(n)}(t)+\sum_{n\leq N-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{l_{2}}^{(n)}(t)\lesssim\delta_{0}\mathcal{D}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)+\mathcal{D}_{N}(t).\end{split}

(2.44)

Here,
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{l_{2}}^{(n)}(t)
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{l_{2}}^{(n)}(t)
are given in (
2.36
).

Proof.

The core idea to prove Lemma
2.7
is parallel to
Step 2
in the proof of Lemma
2.6
.
In what follows, we only establish some distinct and critical estimates, and the analogous calculations will be omitted for simplicity.
Indeed, notice that the estimates of
\mathcal{J}_{7,2}
and
\mathcal{J}_{8,2}
in the case of
|\alpha|+|\beta|=N
include the term

\displaystyle\delta_{0}\sum_{n\leq N-1}(1+t)^{\sigma_{n,|\beta|+1}}\mathcal{D}_{l_{2}}^{n,|\beta|+1}(t)=\delta_{0}\sum_{n\leq N-1}(1+t)^{\sigma_{N,|\beta|}}\mathcal{D}_{l_{2}}^{n,|\beta|+1}(t).

(2.45)

Obviously, this result (
2.45
) is out of control when
|\alpha|+|\beta|=n<N
due to (
2.37
). Thus, we should to revise the estimates of
\mathcal{J}_{7,2}
and
\mathcal{J}_{8,2}
as follows. Direct calculation leads to

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{J}_{7,2}=\;&\sum_{0\leq|\alpha^{\prime}|\leq N_{0}}\Big\langle-v\times\partial_{x}^{\alpha^{\prime}}B^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon},\widetilde{w}^{2}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\\
\;&+\sum_{N_{0}+1\leq|\alpha^{\prime}|\leq|\alpha|\leq n}\Big\langle-v\times\partial_{x}^{\alpha^{\prime}}B^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon},\widetilde{w}^{2}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\\
\equiv\;&\mathcal{J}_{7,2}^{1}+\mathcal{J}_{7,2}^{2}.\end{split}

Here
N_{0}\geq 3
is given in (
1.24
).
Applying the Hölder inequality, the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality with
\eta
, the Sobolev embedding
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\hookrightarrow L^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and (
2.23
), we derive

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{J}_{7,2}^{1}}\lesssim\;&\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}^{2}\sum_{|\alpha^{\prime}|=1}\|\langle v\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(|\alpha|-1,|\beta|+1)\nabla_{v}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\\
\;&+\varepsilon^{2}\sum_{2\leq|\alpha^{\prime}|\leq N_{0}-1}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha^{\prime}}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{3}_{x}}^{2}\|\langle v\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(|\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}|+1,|\beta|+1)\nabla_{v}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{6}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}\\
\;&+\varepsilon^{2}\sum_{|\alpha^{\prime}|=N_{0}}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha^{\prime}}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\|\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(|\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}|+1,|\beta|+1)\langle v\rangle^{-\frac{7}{2}}\nabla_{v}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}\\
\;&+\frac{\eta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\\
\lesssim\;&\varepsilon^{3}\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{N_{0}-1}_{x}}^{2}(1+t)^{\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}}\sum_{n\leq N-1}(1+t)^{\sigma_{n,|\beta|+1}}\mathcal{D}_{l_{2}}^{n,|\beta|+1}(t)+\eta(1+t)^{\sigma_{n,|\beta|}}\mathcal{D}_{l_{2}}^{n,|\beta|}(t)\\
\lesssim\;&\delta_{0}\sum_{n\leq N-1}(1+t)^{\sigma_{n,|\beta|}}\mathcal{D}_{l_{2}}^{n,|\beta|+1}(t)+\eta(1+t)^{\sigma_{n,|\beta|}}\mathcal{D}_{l_{2}}^{n,|\beta|}(t),\end{split}

where we have used the
a priori
assumption (
2.2
) and the facts that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&\langle v\rangle\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\lesssim\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(|\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}|+1,|\beta|+1)\langle v\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\quad\quad\quad\text{in the case of}\quad 2\leq|\alpha^{\prime}|\leq N_{0}-1,\\
\;&\langle v\rangle\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\lesssim\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(|\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}|+1,|\beta|+1)\langle v\rangle^{-\frac{7}{2}}\quad\quad\;\,\text{in the case of}\quad|\alpha^{\prime}|=N_{0}.\end{split}

For the term
{\mathcal{J}_{7,2}^{2}}
, it can be inferred from the Hölder inequality, the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, (
2.23
) and the
a priori
assumption (
2.2
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{J}_{7,2}^{2}}\lesssim\;&\!\!\!\!\sum_{N_{0}+1\leq|\alpha^{\prime}|\leq N}\!\!\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha^{\prime}}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\langle v\rangle\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\nabla_{v}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\|\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\\
\lesssim\;&\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{N-1}_{x}}^{2}\sum_{N_{0}+1\leq|\alpha^{\prime}|\leq N}\|\langle v\rangle\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\nabla_{v}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}\\
\;&+\frac{\eta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\\
\lesssim\;&\delta_{0}{\overline{\mathcal{D}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)+\eta(1+t)^{\sigma_{n,|\beta|}}\mathcal{D}_{l_{2}}^{n,|\beta|}(t).\end{split}

Here, we have used the inequality

\displaystyle\begin{split}\langle v\rangle\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(\alpha,\beta)\leq\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(|\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}|+2,|\beta|+1)\langle v\rangle^{l_{2}-l_{1}-4|\alpha^{\prime}|+8+\frac{1}{2}|\beta|+4}\leq\overline{w}_{l_{1}}(|\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}|+2,|\beta|+1),\end{split}

which is derived from

1+l_{2}-l_{1}-4|\alpha^{\prime}|+8+\frac{1}{2}|\beta|+4\leq l_{2}-l_{1}+9+\frac{1}{2}N-\frac{9}{2}N_{0}\leq 0
because of
|\alpha^{\prime}|\geq N_{0}+1,|\beta|=n-|\alpha^{\prime}|\leq N-N_{0}
and (
1.24
).
Thereby, combining the estimates of
{\mathcal{J}_{7,2}^{1}}
and
{\mathcal{J}_{7,2}^{2}}
gives rise to

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{J}_{7,2}\lesssim\;&\delta_{0}\sum_{n\leq N-1}(1+t)^{\sigma_{n,|\beta|}}\mathcal{D}_{l_{2}}^{n,|\beta|+1}(t)+\delta_{0}{\overline{\mathcal{D}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)+\eta(1+t)^{\sigma_{n,|\beta|}}\mathcal{D}_{l_{2}}^{n,|\beta|}(t).\end{split}

By employing the analogous argument of the estimate of
\mathcal{J}_{7,2}
, we are also able to deduce

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{J}_{8,2}\lesssim\;&\delta_{0}\sum_{n\leq N-1}(1+t)^{\sigma_{n,|\beta|}}\mathcal{D}_{l_{2}}^{n,|\beta|+1}(t)+\delta_{0}{\overline{\mathcal{D}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)+\eta(1+t)^{\sigma_{n,|\beta|}}\mathcal{D}_{l_{2}}^{n,|\beta|}(t).\end{split}

Furthermore, for the rest terms in (
2.32
) when
|\alpha|+|\beta|=n\leq N-1
, taking the similar estimated manner as
Step 2
in the proof of Lemma
2.6
, and then combining the above estimates of
\mathcal{J}_{7,2}
and
\mathcal{J}_{8,2}
, we conclude (
2.44
). This completes the proof of Lemma
2.7
.
∎

Finally, with Lemma
2.6
and Lemma
2.7
in hand, we turn to completing the proof of Proposition
2.5
.

Proof of Proposition
2.5
.

From (
1.18
) and (
2.36
),
we deduce

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{N,l_{2}}(t)=\sum_{n\leq N}\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{l_{2}}^{(n)}(t),\quad\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{N,l_{2}}(t)=\sum_{n\leq N}\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{l_{2}}^{(n)}(t).

Therefore, gathering (
2.38
) and(
2.44
), and then choosing
\delta_{0}
small enough, we conclude (
2.35
). This completes the proof of Proposition
2.5
.
∎

2.3.
Energy Estimate in Negative Sobolev Space

This subsection aims to provide an energy estimate in negative Sobolev space for the VMB system (
1.2
).

Proposition 2.8
.

Assume that the
a priori
assumption (
2.2
) holds true for
\delta_{0}
small enough.
Then, there holds

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\mathcal{E}_{-s}(t)+\mathcal{D}_{-s}(t)+\varepsilon\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\mathcal{E}^{-s}_{int}(t)\lesssim\mathcal{D}_{N}(t)+\delta_{0}\mathcal{D}_{N}(t)+\delta_{0}{\overline{\mathcal{D}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t).\end{split}

(2.46)

Here,
\mathcal{E}_{-s}(t)
and
\mathcal{D}_{-s}(t)
are given in (
1.20
) and (
1.21
), while
\mathcal{E}^{-s}_{int}(t)
satisfies (
2.49
).

Proof of Proposition
2.8
.

The proof of (
2.46
) will be split up into two steps.

Step 1. Energy estimate of
\Lambda^{-s}f^{\varepsilon}
.

Precisely speaking, we can obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\left[\left\|\Lambda^{-s}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\left\|\Lambda^{-s}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\left\|\Lambda^{-s}B^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\right]+\frac{\sigma_{0}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\\
\lesssim\;&\delta_{0}\left[\varepsilon^{2}\|\Lambda^{\frac{3}{4}-\frac{s}{2}}E^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\Lambda^{\frac{3}{4}-\frac{s}{2}}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\Lambda^{\frac{3}{2}-s}E^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\Lambda^{\frac{3}{2}-s}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\right]\\
\;&+\delta_{0}\left[\|\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|\Lambda^{1-s}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\mathcal{D}_{N}(t)+{\overline{\mathcal{D}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)\right].\end{split}

(2.47)

To do this,
applying
\Lambda^{-s}
to the first equation of (
1.2
) and taking the inner product with
\Lambda^{-s}f^{\varepsilon}
over
\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}
, we derive from (
1.11
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\left\|\Lambda^{-s}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\underbrace{\left\langle\Lambda^{-s}E^{\varepsilon}\cdot v\mu^{1/2},\Lambda^{-s}f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}_{\mathcal{I}_{1}}+\frac{\sigma_{0}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}^{2}\\
=\;&\underbrace{\left\langle-\varepsilon\Lambda^{-s}\left(E^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\right),\Lambda^{-s}f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}+\underbrace{\left\langle\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\Lambda^{-s}\left(E^{\varepsilon}\cdot vf^{\varepsilon}\right),\Lambda^{-s}f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}_{\mathcal{I}_{3}}\\
\;&+\underbrace{\left\langle-\Lambda^{-s}\left(v\times B^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\right),\Lambda^{-s}f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}_{\mathcal{I}_{4}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\langle\Lambda^{-s}\Gamma(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}),\Lambda^{-s}f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}_{\mathcal{I}_{5}}.\end{split}

(2.48)

Now we further estimate
\mathcal{I}_{1}\sim\mathcal{I}_{5}
in (
2.48
) term by term. It follows from the second and third equations of (
1.2
) that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}=\left\langle\Lambda^{-s}E^{\varepsilon},\Lambda^{-s}\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}-\nabla_{x}\times B^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x}}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\left(\left\|\Lambda^{-s}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\left\|\Lambda^{-s}B^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\right).

Owing to
1<s<\frac{3}{2}
, by using (
A.7
), (
A.8
), the Minkowski inequality (
A.9
), the Hölder inequality, the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality with
\eta
and the
a priori
assumption (
2.2
), we deduce

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{I}_{2}=\;&\varepsilon\left\langle\Lambda^{-s}\left(E^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\right),\Lambda^{-s}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\varepsilon\left\langle\Lambda^{-s}\left(E^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\right),\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\\
\lesssim\;&\varepsilon\|\Lambda^{-s}(E^{\varepsilon}\mu^{\delta}f^{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|\Lambda^{-s}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\varepsilon\|\Lambda^{-s}(E^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\langle v\rangle^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\\
\lesssim\;&\varepsilon\left\|E^{\varepsilon}\mu^{\delta}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{{L^{2}_{v}}{L^{\frac{6}{3+2s}}_{x}}}\left\|\Lambda^{-s}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\varepsilon\|E^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\langle v\rangle^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{v}L_{x}^{\frac{6}{3+2s}}}\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\\
\lesssim\;&\varepsilon\left\|E^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{\frac{12}{3+2s}}_{x}}\left\|\mu^{\delta}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{v}{L^{\frac{12}{3+2s}}_{x}}}\left\|\Lambda^{-s}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\varepsilon\|E^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{s}}_{x}}\|\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\left\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\\
\lesssim\;&\delta_{0}\varepsilon\|\Lambda^{\frac{3}{4}-\frac{s}{2}}E^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\Lambda^{\frac{3}{4}-\frac{s}{2}}(\mu^{\delta}f^{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\varepsilon\|\Lambda^{\frac{3}{2}-s}E^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\left\|\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\left\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{{}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}}\\
\lesssim\;&\delta_{0}\left[\varepsilon^{2}\|\Lambda^{\frac{3}{4}-\frac{s}{2}}E^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\Lambda^{\frac{3}{4}-\frac{s}{2}}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\right]+\delta_{0}\varepsilon^{4}\|\Lambda^{\frac{3}{2}-s}E^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\frac{\eta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{{}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}}.\end{split}

Similarly, we directly find that
\mathcal{I}_{3}
has the same upper bound as
\mathcal{I}_{2}
.

For
\mathcal{I}_{4}
, we derive from (
A.7
), (
A.8
), the Minkowski inequality (
A.9
), the Hölder inequality, the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality with
\eta
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{I}_{4}=\;&\left\langle\Lambda^{-s}\left(v\times B^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\right),\Lambda^{-s}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\left\langle\Lambda^{-s}\left(v\times B^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\right),\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\\
\lesssim\;&\|\Lambda^{-1-s}(B^{\varepsilon}\mu^{\delta}f^{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|\Lambda^{1-s}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|\Lambda^{-s}(v\times B^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\\
\lesssim\;&\|B^{\varepsilon}\mu^{\delta}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{{L^{2}_{v}}{L^{\frac{6}{5+2s}}_{x}}}\|\Lambda^{1-s}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|v\times B^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{v}L_{x}^{\frac{6}{3+2s}}}\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\\
\lesssim\;&\left\|B^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{\frac{3}{1+s}}_{x}}\|\mu^{\delta}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|\Lambda^{1-s}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{s}}_{x}}\|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\\
\lesssim\;&\|\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}\!-\!s}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\mu^{\delta}\!f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|\!\Lambda^{1-s}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\!\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\!+\!\|\Lambda^{\frac{3}{2}-s}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}\!-\!\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{{}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}}\\
\lesssim\;&\delta_{0}\left[\|\mu^{\delta}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\|\Lambda^{1-s}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\right]+\delta_{0}\left[\|\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}^{2}\right]+\eta\mathcal{D}_{-s}(t).\end{split}

Here, deduced from the interpolation inequality with respect to the spatial derivatives, the
a priori
assumption (
2.2
) and
1<s<\frac{3}{2}
, the facts utilized are that

\displaystyle\|\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}-s}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq\|\Lambda^{-s}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\delta_{0},\quad\|\Lambda^{\frac{3}{2}-s}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq\|B^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}_{x}}\lesssim\delta_{0}.

Finally, for the term
\mathcal{I}_{5}
, by the collision invariant property, we arrive at

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{I}_{5}\lesssim\;&\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|\Lambda^{-s}(\langle v\rangle^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}}\Gamma(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}))\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\\
\lesssim\;&\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|\langle v\rangle^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}}\Gamma(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}_{v}L_{x}^{\frac{6}{3+2s}}}\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\\
\lesssim\;&\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|\langle v\rangle^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}}\Gamma(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon})\|_{L_{x}^{\frac{6}{3+2s}}L^{2}_{v}}\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\\
\lesssim\;&\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\||f^{\varepsilon}|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}|f^{\varepsilon}|_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\|_{L_{x}^{\frac{6}{3+2s}}}\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\\
\lesssim\;&\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{x}^{\frac{3}{s}}L^{2}_{v}}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{2}_{v}}\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\\
\lesssim\;&\delta_{0}\|\Lambda^{\frac{3}{2}-s}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}^{2},\end{split}

where we have used (
A.7
), (
A.8
), the Minkowski inequality (
A.9
), the Hölder inequality, the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality with
\eta
and (
2.2
) .

As a result, gathering the above estimates of
\mathcal{I}_{1}\sim\mathcal{I}_{5}
into
(
2.48
)
and choosing
\eta
small enough yield
(
2.47
).

Step 2. Macroscopic dissipation estimate of
\Lambda^{1-s}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}
.

In fact, similar to Lemma
2.2
, there exists an interactive functional
\mathcal{E}^{-s}_{int}(t)
defined by
satisfying

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{E}^{-s}_{int}(t)\right|\lesssim\left\|\Lambda^{-s}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\left\|\Lambda^{1-s}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\left\|\Lambda^{1-s}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\left\|\Lambda^{1-s}\nabla_{x}\times B^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}},

(2.49)

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varepsilon\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\mathcal{E}^{-s}_{int}(t)+\left\|\Lambda^{1-s}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\varepsilon^{2}\left\|\Lambda^{1-s}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\varepsilon^{2}\left\|\Lambda^{1-s}\nabla_{x}\times B^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim\;&\left\|\Lambda^{1-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\eta\|\Lambda^{1-s}\nabla_{x}(a^{\varepsilon}+c^{\varepsilon})\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\;&+\eta\varepsilon^{2}\left\|\Lambda^{1-s}\nabla_{x}\times E^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\varepsilon^{2}\left(\|\Lambda^{-s}\mathcal{X}_{b^{\varepsilon}}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}}^{2}+\|\Lambda^{1-s}\mathcal{X}_{b^{\varepsilon}}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}}^{2}\right)\\
\;&+\varepsilon^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\sum_{j\neq i}\left\|\big\langle\Lambda^{-s}(\mathcal{G}_{3}^{i}+\mathcal{G}_{3}^{j}+\mathcal{G}_{4}^{i,j}+\mathcal{G}_{5}^{i}),\,\zeta\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(2.50)

Recalling (
2.7
), (
2.10
) for
g^{\varepsilon}
and
\mathcal{X}_{b^{\varepsilon}}
, we obtain from (
A.3
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varepsilon^{2}\left(\|\Lambda^{-s}\mathcal{X}_{b^{\varepsilon}}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}}^{2}\!+\!\|\Lambda^{1-s}\mathcal{X}_{b^{\varepsilon}}\|_{L_{x,v}^{2}}^{2}\right)\!+\!\varepsilon^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\!\sum_{j\neq i}\!\left\|\big\langle\Lambda^{-s}(\mathcal{G}_{3}^{i}+\mathcal{G}_{3}^{j}+\mathcal{G}_{4}^{i,j}+\mathcal{G}_{5}^{i}),\,\zeta\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim\;&\left\|\Lambda^{1-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}^{2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}^{2}+\mathcal{D}_{N}(t)+\delta_{0}\left\|\Lambda^{1-s}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\\
\;&+\delta_{0}\left[\left\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}^{2}+\mathcal{D}_{N}(t)\right].\end{split}

(2.51)

Hence,
combining (
2.50
) and (
2.51
) and choosing
\delta_{0}
small enough, we conclude

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varepsilon\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\mathcal{E}^{-s}_{int}(t)+\left\|\Lambda^{1-s}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\varepsilon^{2}\left\|\Lambda^{1-s}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\varepsilon^{2}\left\|\Lambda^{1-s}\nabla_{x}\times B^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim\;&\left\|\Lambda^{1-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\eta\|\Lambda^{1-s}\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x.v}}\\
\;&+\eta\varepsilon^{2}\left\|\Lambda^{1-s}\nabla_{x}\times E^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\mathcal{D}_{N}(t)+\delta_{0}\left[\left\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}^{2}+\mathcal{D}_{N}(t)\right].\end{split}

(2.52)

Moreover,
via the interpolation inequality with respect to the spatial derivatives, it is easy to derive that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|\Lambda^{1-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\lesssim\left\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}^{2}+\left\|\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}^{2},\\
&\left\|\Lambda^{\frac{3}{4}-\frac{s}{2}}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}^{2}\lesssim\left\|\Lambda^{1-s}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\left\|\Lambda^{-s}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}^{2}+\left\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}^{2},\\
&\left\|\Lambda^{\frac{3}{2}-s}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\lesssim\left\|\Lambda^{1-s}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\left\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}^{2},\\
&\left\|\Lambda^{1-s}\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\left\|\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\lesssim\left\|\Lambda^{1-s}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2},\\
&\left\|\Lambda^{\frac{3}{4}-\frac{s}{2}}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\left\|\Lambda^{\frac{3}{2}-s}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\left\|\Lambda^{1-s}\nabla_{x}\times E^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\left\|\Lambda^{1-s}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{H^{1}_{x}}^{2},\end{split}

(2.53)

where we have used
\frac{3}{4}-\frac{s}{2}>1-s
for
1<s<\frac{3}{2}
.
Hence, with the aid of (
2.53
),
we deduce (
2.46
) by a proper linear combination of (
2.47
) and (
2.52
). This completes the proof of Proposition
2.8
.
∎

3.
Global Existence

In this section, we are devoted to the proof of Theorem
1.1
by first establishing the closed uniform estimates on
X(t)
defined in (
2.1
).
For this purpose, we also need to obtain the time decay estimates of
\varepsilon^{s}\mathcal{E}_{N_{0}}^{0}(t)
and
\varepsilon^{1+s}\mathcal{E}_{N_{0}}^{1}(t)
.

3.1.
Time Decay Estimate

Our main idea to deduce the time decay estimate is based on the approach proposed by
[
26
]
. We are now in a position to state the main result of this subsection.

Proposition 3.1
.

Assume that the
a priori
assumption (
2.2
) holds true for
\delta_{0}
small enough.
There exist two energy functionals
\mathcal{E}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t)
,
\mathcal{E}^{1}_{{}^{N_{0}}}(t)
and the corresponding energy dissipation rate functionals
\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t)
,
\mathcal{D}^{1}_{N_{0}}(t)
satisfying (
1.22
) and (
1.23
) respectively such that
for all
0\leq t\leq T

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\mathcal{E}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t)+\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t)\leq 0,

(3.1)

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\mathcal{E}^{1}_{N_{0}}(t)+\mathcal{D}^{1}_{N_{0}}(t)\lesssim\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{N_{0}-1}_{x}}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t).

(3.2)

Furthermore, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\!\left\{\varepsilon^{s}(1+t)^{\frac{s}{2}}\mathcal{E}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t)\!+\!\varepsilon^{1\!+\!s}(1+t)^{\frac{1\!+\!s}{2}}\mathcal{E}^{1}_{N_{0}}(t)\right\}\lesssim\;&{\mathcal{E}}_{N}(0)\!+\!\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\!\left\{{\mathcal{E}}_{N_{0}\!+\!\frac{1+s}{2}}(t)+{\mathcal{E}}_{\!-s}(t)\right\}.\end{split}

(3.3)

To prove Proposition
3.1
,
we give a more refined energy estimate for the pure spatial derivatives than that of Lemma
2.3
for preparations.

Lemma 3.2
.

Assume that the
a priori
assumption (
2.2
) holds true for
\delta_{0}
small enough. Then, for all
0\leq t\leq T
, there hold

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\left[\left\|f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{H^{N_{0}}_{x}\!L^{2}_{v}}^{2}+\left\|E^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{H^{N_{0}}_{x}}^{2}+\left\|B^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{H^{N_{0}}_{x}}^{2}\right]+\frac{\sigma_{0}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{H^{N_{0}}_{x}\!L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\delta_{0}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t),

(3.4)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\left[\left\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{H^{N_{0}-1}_{x}\!L^{2}_{v}}^{2}\!+\!\left\|\nabla_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{H^{N_{0}-1}_{x}}^{2}\!+\!\left\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{H^{N_{0}-1}_{x}}^{2}\right]+\frac{\sigma_{0}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\|\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}\!-\!\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{H^{N_{0}-1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\\[2.84526pt]
\lesssim\;&\delta_{0}\mathcal{D}^{1}_{N_{0}}(t)+\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{N_{0}-1}_{x}}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t).\end{split}

(3.5)

Proof.

Applying
\nabla^{k}_{x}
with
1\leq k\leq N_{0}
to the first equation of (
1.2
) and taking the inner product with
\nabla^{k}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}
over
\mathbb{R}_{x}^{3}

\times

\mathbb{R}_{v}^{3}
, we obtain by employing the coercivity of
L
in (
1.11
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\left[\left\|\nabla^{k}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\left\|\nabla^{k}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\left\|\nabla^{k}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\right]+\frac{\sigma_{0}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left\|\nabla^{k}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\\
\lesssim\;&\underbrace{\sum_{1\leq j\leq k}\Big\langle\varepsilon\nabla_{x}^{j}E^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{k-j}f^{\varepsilon},\nabla^{k}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}+\underbrace{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\sum_{0\leq j\leq k}\Big\langle v\cdot\nabla^{j}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\nabla^{k-j}_{x}f^{\varepsilon},\nabla^{k}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}\\
&+\underbrace{\sum_{1\leq j\leq k}\Big\langle v\times\nabla^{j}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}\nabla^{k-j}_{x}f^{\varepsilon},\nabla^{k}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}_{\mathcal{M}_{3}}+\underbrace{\Big\langle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla^{k}_{x}\Gamma(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}),\nabla^{k}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}_{\mathcal{M}_{4}}.\end{split}

(3.6)

Here, we have used the collision invariant property.

Now we estimate
\mathcal{M}_{1}\sim\mathcal{M}_{4}
with
1\leq k\leq N_{0}
term by term.
For the term
\mathcal{M}_{1}
, by employing the Hölder inequality, the Sobolev inequalities (
2.23
) and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality with
\eta
, we obtain that for any
2\leq k\leq N_{0}

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{M}_{1}\lesssim\;&\varepsilon\Big[\|\nabla_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{6}_{x}}\|\nabla_{v}\nabla^{k-1}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{3}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{2\leq j\leq N_{0}-1}\!\!\!\!\!\|\nabla^{j}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\nabla_{v}\nabla^{k-j}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\!\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\Big]\|\nabla^{k}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\\
&\quad+\varepsilon\|\nabla^{N_{0}}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\left[\|\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|\nabla_{v}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\right]\left\|\nabla^{N_{0}}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\\
\lesssim\;&{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)\!\!\!\!\sum_{2\leq\ell\leq N_{0}-1}\!\!\!\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla^{\ell}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\!+\!\eta\!\!\!\sum_{2\leq\ell\leq N_{0}}\!\!\!\|\nabla^{\ell}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\!+\!\left[\mathcal{E}_{N}(t)\!+\!{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)\right]\mathcal{D}_{N_{0}}^{1}(t)\\
\lesssim\;&\delta_{0}\mathcal{D}_{N_{0}}^{1}(t)+\eta\mathcal{D}_{N_{0}}^{1}(t).\end{split}

(3.7)

Here, we have used the
a priori
assumption (
2.2
) and the fact, which is deduced from the interpolation inequality (
A.3
) and the relation of
N-N_{0}\geq 2
because of (
1.24
), that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\left\|\nabla^{N_{0}}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\left[\left\|\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla_{v}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\left\|\nabla^{N_{0}-1}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{2(N-N_{0})}{N-N_{0}+1}}\left\|\nabla^{N}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{\frac{2}{N-N_{0}+1}}\Big[\left\|\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\left\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\!+\!\left\|\nabla_{v}^{3}\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{\frac{1}{3}}

\displaystyle\times\left\|\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{\frac{2}{3}}\left\|\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{2}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{E}_{N}(t)+{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)\right]\mathcal{D}_{N_{0}}^{1}(t).

As for the case of
k=1
, similar to that of (
3.7
),
one has that

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{1}\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\varepsilon\left\|\nabla_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{6}_{x}}\left\|\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{3}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\left\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\varepsilon\|\nabla_{x}^{2}E^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\nabla_{v}^{3}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{\frac{1}{6}}\|\nabla_{v}^{3}\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{\frac{1}{6}}\left\|f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{\frac{1}{3}}\left\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{\frac{4}{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\varepsilon\left\|\nabla_{x}^{2}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\left\|\nabla_{v}^{3}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{\frac{1}{6}}\|\nabla_{v}^{3}\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{\frac{1}{6}}\|\Lambda^{-1}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{\frac{2}{9}}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{\frac{1}{9}}\|\Lambda^{-1}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{\frac{4}{9}}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{\frac{8}{9}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\big[{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{-s}(t)\big]\varepsilon^{2}\left\|\nabla_{x}^{2}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\eta\left\|\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\delta_{0}\mathcal{D}_{N_{0}}^{1}(t)+\eta\mathcal{D}_{N_{0}}^{1}(t).

In an analogous manner,
\mathcal{M}_{2}
has the same upper bound as
\mathcal{M}_{1}
.

For
\mathcal{M}_{3}
, utilizing (
2.3
) leads to

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{3}=\;

\displaystyle\underbrace{\sum_{1\leq j\leq k}\Big\langle v\times\nabla^{j}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}\nabla^{k-j}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon},\nabla^{k}_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}_{\mathcal{M}_{3,1}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{1\leq j\leq k}\Big\langle v\times\nabla^{j}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}\nabla^{k-j}_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon},\nabla^{k}_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}_{\mathcal{M}_{3,2}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{1\leq j\leq k}\Big\langle v\times\nabla^{j}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}\nabla^{k-j}_{x}f^{\varepsilon},\nabla^{k}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{x,v}}}_{\mathcal{M}_{3,3}}

It follows from the Hölder inequality, the Sobolev embedding theory and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality with
\eta
that for
1\leq k\leq N_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{3,1}\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\!\Big[\!\!\sum_{1\leq j\leq N_{0}-1}\!\!\!\!\!\!\|\nabla^{j}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{3}_{x}}\|\nabla^{k-j}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\!\|_{L^{6}_{x}\!L^{2}_{v}}\!+\!\|\nabla^{N_{0}}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|(\mathbf{I}\!-\!\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}\!L^{2}_{v}}\Big]\|\nabla^{k}_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,\!v}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{N}(t)\sum_{1\leq\ell\leq N_{0}}\|\nabla^{\ell}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}^{2}+\eta\|\nabla^{k}_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}.

In a similar manner, for
1\leq k\leq N_{0}
, we find that

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{3,2}\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\!\Big[\sum_{1\leq j\leq N_{0}-1}\|\nabla^{j}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{3}_{x}}\|\nabla^{k-j}_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\!\|_{L^{6}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\!\|\nabla^{N_{0}}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\Big]\|\nabla^{k}_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{N_{0}-1}_{x}}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t).

Applying the Hölder inequality and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality with
\eta
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{3,3}\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\Big[\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{6}_{x}}\|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\nabla^{k-1}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{3}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\sum_{2\leq j\leq N_{0}-2}\|\nabla^{j}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{6}_{x}}\|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\nabla^{k-j}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{3}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle+\|\nabla^{N_{0}-1}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\|\nabla^{N_{0}}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\Big]\|\nabla^{k}_{x}(\mathbf{I}\!-\!\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\left[{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{N}(t)\right]\varepsilon^{2}\sum_{3\leq\ell\leq N_{0}-1}\|\nabla^{\ell}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\left[\mathcal{E}_{N}(t)+{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{-s}(t)\right]\mathcal{D}_{N_{0}}^{1}(t)

\displaystyle+\frac{\eta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\nabla^{k}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}

holds for
2\leq k\leq N_{0}
. Here, we have used the facts, which are derived from (
2.3
), the interpolation inequality (
A.5
) and the relation of
N-N_{0}\geq 2
because of (
1.24
), that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{6}_{x}}^{2}\|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\nabla^{k-1}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{3}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{6}_{x}}^{2}\|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\nabla^{k-1}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{3}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{6}_{x}}^{2}\|\nabla^{k-1}_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{3}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\left\|\Lambda^{-1}B^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\nabla_{x}^{3}B^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{\frac{3}{2}}\|\langle v\rangle^{2}\nabla_{v}\nabla^{k-1}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\|\nabla_{v}^{2}\nabla^{k-1}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\|\nabla^{k-1}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{N_{0}-1}_{x}}^{2}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t)

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\big[{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{-s}(t)\big]\mathcal{D}_{N_{0}}^{1}(t)+\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{N_{0}-1}_{x}}^{2}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t),

and

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\|\nabla^{N_{0}}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\left\|\nabla^{N_{0}}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\left[\left\|\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}+\left\|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\left\|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{2}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\left\|\nabla^{N_{0}-1}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{2(N-N_{0})}{N-N_{0}+1}}\left\|\nabla^{N}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{\frac{2}{N-N_{0}+1}}\Big[\left\|\nabla_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\left\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|\nabla_{v}^{3}\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{\frac{1}{3}}\left\|\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{\frac{2}{3}}\left\|\langle v\rangle^{2}\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{H^{1}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{E}_{N}(t)+{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)\right]\mathcal{D}_{N_{0}}^{1}(t).

In the case of
k=1
, by employing similar argument, we can deduce

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{3,3}\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\Big[\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\langle v\rangle\nabla_{v}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{3}_{x}}\|\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{6}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\Big]\|\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\big[~{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{-s}(t)\big]\mathcal{D}_{N_{0}}^{1}(t)+\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{N_{0}-1}_{x}}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t).

Consequently, gathering the above estimates of
\mathcal{M}_{3,i}(i=1,2,3)

and using the
a priori
assumption (
2.2
) ,
we arrive at the following outcome

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{3}\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\delta_{0}\mathcal{D}^{1}_{N_{0}}(t)+\eta\mathcal{D}^{1}_{N_{0}}(t)+\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{N_{0}-1}_{x}}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t).

For the term
\mathcal{M}_{4}
, making use of the Hölder inequality, the Sobolev inequality (
2.23
) and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{4}\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Big[\!\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\|\nabla_{x}^{k}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\!\sum_{1\leq j\leq k-1}\!\!\|\nabla^{k-j}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{3}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\|\nabla^{j}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{6}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\!\Big]\|\nabla^{k}_{x}(\mathbf{I}\!-\!\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{1/2}_{N}(t)\Big\{\sum_{2\leq\ell\leq N_{0}}\|\nabla^{\ell}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}^{2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\nabla^{k}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\delta_{0}\mathcal{D}_{N_{0}}^{1}(t)\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\,\,\,\text{for}\quad k\geq 2,

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{4}\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\|f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{3}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\left\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{6}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\left\|\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\lesssim\delta_{0}\mathcal{D}_{N_{0}}^{1}(t)\quad\text{for}\quad k=1.

Therefore, putting all the estimates mentioned above into
(
3.6
) and summing the resulting inequality over
1\leq k\leq N_{0}
, we directly conclude (
3.5
).
Furthermore, under the
a priori
assumption (
2.2
), by combining (
2.24
) and (
3.5
), the desired estimate (
3.4
) follows.
This completes the proof of Lemma
3.2
.
∎

Now we complete the Proof of Proposition
3.1
.

Proof of Proposition
3.1
.

We divide it into two steps.

Step 1. The uniform energy estimate
\mathcal{E}^{k}_{N_{0}}(t)
with
k=0,1
.

Similar to Lemma
2.2
, by considering the
a priori
assumption (
2.2
), we obtain that
there exists an interactive energy functional
\mathcal{E}^{N_{0}}_{int}(t)
satisfying

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|\mathcal{E}^{N_{0}}_{int}(t)\right|\lesssim\;&\sum_{|\alpha|\leq N_{0}}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq N_{0}}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\sum_{|\alpha|\leq N_{0}}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}},\end{split}

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varepsilon\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\mathcal{E}^{N_{0}}_{int}(t)+\sum_{1\leq k\leq N_{0}}\!\!\|\nabla^{k}_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\sum_{1\leq k\leq N_{0}-1}\|\nabla^{k}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\sum_{2\leq k\leq N_{0}-1}\!\!\|\nabla^{k}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\\
\lesssim\;&\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq N_{0}}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\delta_{0}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t)+\eta\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t).\end{split}

(3.8)

Therefore, by recalling (
1.22
) for
\mathcal{E}^{k}_{N_{0}}(t)
when
k=0
and combining the energy estimate (
3.4
) and the macroscopic estimate (
3.8
),
we conclude (
3.1
).

Further,
applying the analogous argument as Lemma
2.2
,
we can also derive that
there exists an interactive energy functional
\mathcal{E}^{1\rightarrow N_{0}}_{int}(t)
satisfying

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|\mathcal{E}^{1\rightarrow N_{0}}_{int}(t)\right|\lesssim\;&\sum_{1\leq|\alpha|\leq N_{0}}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\sum_{1\leq|\alpha|\leq N_{0}}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\sum_{1\leq|\alpha|\leq N_{0}}\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}},\end{split}

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varepsilon\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\mathcal{E}^{1\rightarrow N_{0}}_{int}(t)+\sum_{2\leq k\leq N_{0}}\!\!\|\nabla^{k}_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\!\!\sum_{2\leq k\leq N_{0}-1}\!\!\|\nabla^{k}_{x}E^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\sum_{3\leq k\leq N_{0}-1}\!\!\|\nabla^{k}_{x}B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\\
\lesssim\;&\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\!\!\sum_{1\leq|\alpha|\leq N_{0}}\!\!\left\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}+\delta_{0}\mathcal{D}^{1}_{N_{0}}(t)+\eta\mathcal{D}^{1}_{N_{0}}(t).\end{split}

(3.9)

Consequently, recalling (
1.22
) for
\mathcal{E}^{k}_{N}(t)
when
k=1
, the desired estimate (
3.2
) follows from the proper linear combination of (
3.5
) and (
3.9
).

Step 2. The time decay estimate (
3.3
).

Indeed, from Lemma
A.3
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}_{x}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{\frac{s}{2+s}}\left\|\Lambda^{-s}\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{\frac{2}{2+s}},

\displaystyle\left\|[{E}^{\varepsilon},\,{B}^{\varepsilon}]\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-\frac{s}{2+s}}\left\|\nabla^{2}_{x}[\varepsilon{E}^{\varepsilon},\,\varepsilon{B}^{\varepsilon}]\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{\frac{s}{2+s}}\left\|\Lambda^{-s}[{E}^{\varepsilon},\,{B}^{\varepsilon}]\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{\frac{2}{2+s}},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{x}{B}^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-\frac{s}{2+s}}\left[\varepsilon\left\|\nabla^{2}_{x}{B}^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\right]^{\frac{s}{2+s}}\|\Lambda^{1-s/2}{B}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{\frac{2}{2+s}},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{x}^{N_{0}}[{E}^{\varepsilon},\,{B}^{\varepsilon}]\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-\frac{s}{2+s}}\left\|\nabla^{N_{0}-1}_{x}[\varepsilon{E}^{\varepsilon},\,\varepsilon{B}^{\varepsilon}]\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{\frac{s}{2+s}}\|\nabla^{N_{0}+\frac{s}{2}}_{x}[{E}^{\varepsilon},\,{B}^{\varepsilon}]\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{\frac{2}{2+s}}.

The above inequalities together with the fact that for
0\leq m\leq N_{0}

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{m}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla^{m}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}(\nu)}\right)^{\frac{s}{2+s}}\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\langle v\rangle^{-\frac{\gamma s}{2}}\nabla^{m}_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\right)^{\frac{2}{2+s}}

imply

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t)\leq\varepsilon^{-\frac{2s}{2+s}}\left\{{\mathcal{E}}_{N_{0}+\frac{s}{2}}(t)+{\mathcal{E}}_{-s}(t)\right\}^{\frac{2}{2+s}}\left\{\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t)\right\}^{\frac{s}{2+s}}.

(3.10)

Furthermore, multiplying (
3.1
) by
\varepsilon^{s}(1+t)^{\frac{s}{2}+p}
and integrating
the resulting inequality with respect to
t
over
[0,t]
,
we arrive at

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{s}(1+t)^{\frac{s}{2}+p}\mathcal{E}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon^{s}(1+\tau)^{\frac{s}{2}+p}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{s}\mathcal{E}^{0}_{N_{0}}(0)+\frac{s}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon^{s}(1+\tau)^{\frac{s}{2}-1+p}\mathcal{E}^{0}_{N_{0}}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim\;

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{N}(0)+\eta\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon^{s}(1+\tau)^{\frac{s}{2}+p}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau+\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\left\{{\mathcal{E}}_{N_{0}+\frac{s}{2}}(\tau)+{\mathcal{E}}_{-s}(\tau)\right\}(1+t)^{p}.

Here, applied
(
3.10
) and the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality with
\eta
, the fact utilized is that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon^{s}(1+\tau)^{\frac{s}{2}+p-1}\mathcal{E}^{0}_{N_{0}}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\\
\lesssim\;&\int_{0}^{t}\left[\varepsilon^{s}(1+\tau)^{\frac{s}{2}+p}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(\tau)\right]^{\frac{s}{2+s}}(1+\tau)^{(\frac{s}{2}+p)\frac{2}{2+s}-1}\left\{{\mathcal{E}}_{N_{0}+\frac{s}{2}}(t)+{\mathcal{E}}_{-s}(t)\right\}^{\frac{2}{2+s}}\mathrm{d}\tau\\
\lesssim\;&\eta\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon^{s}(1+\tau)^{\frac{s}{2}+p}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau+\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\left\{{\mathcal{E}}_{N_{0}+\frac{1+s}{2}}(\tau)+{\mathcal{E}}_{-s}(\tau)\right\}\int_{0}^{t}(1+\tau)^{-1+p}\mathrm{d}\tau\\
\lesssim\;&\eta\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon^{s}(1+\tau)^{\frac{s}{2}+p}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau+\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\left\{{\mathcal{E}}_{N_{0}+\frac{s}{2}}(\tau)+{\mathcal{E}}_{-s}(\tau)\right\}(1+t)^{p}.\end{split}

Consequently, we can obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\left\{\varepsilon^{s}(1+t)^{\frac{s}{2}}\mathcal{E}^{0}_{N_{0}}(t)\right\}+(1+t)^{-p}\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon^{s}(1+\tau)^{\frac{s}{2}+p}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\\
\lesssim\;&\mathcal{E}_{N}(0)+\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\left\{{\mathcal{E}}_{N_{0}+\frac{s}{2}}(\tau)+{\mathcal{E}}_{-s}(\tau)\right\}.\end{split}

(3.11)

On the other hand, multiply (
3.2
) by
\varepsilon^{1+s}(1+t)^{\frac{1+s}{2}+p}
and integrate
the resulting inequality with respect to
t
over
[0,t]
to yield

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&\varepsilon^{1+s}(1+t)^{\frac{1+s}{2}+p}\mathcal{E}^{1}_{N_{0}}(t)+\varepsilon^{1+s}\int_{0}^{t}(1+\tau)^{\frac{1+s}{2}+p}\mathcal{D}^{1}_{N_{0}}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\\
\lesssim\;&\mathcal{E}_{N}(0)\!+\!\varepsilon^{1+s}\!\!\int_{0}^{t}\!\!(1\!+\!\tau)^{\frac{s-1}{2}+p}\mathcal{E}^{1}_{N_{0}}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\!+\!\varepsilon^{1+s}\!\!\int_{0}^{t}\!\!(1+\tau)^{\frac{1+s}{2}+p}\!\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{H^{N_{0}\!-\!1}_{x}}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(\!\tau\!)\mathrm{d}\tau.\end{split}

(3.12)

Applying the analogous argument as the estimate (
3.10
) gives rise to

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{1}_{N_{0}}(t)\leq\varepsilon^{-\frac{2(1+s)}{3+s}}\left\{{\mathcal{E}}_{N_{0}+\frac{1+s}{2}}(t)+{\mathcal{E}}_{-s}(t)\right\}^{\frac{2}{3+s}}\left\{\mathcal{D}^{1}_{N_{0}}(t)\right\}^{\frac{1+s}{3+s}}.

Then the second term on the right-hand side of (
3.12
) can be inferred from the above inequality and the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality with
\eta
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&\varepsilon^{1+s}\int_{0}^{t}(1+\tau)^{\frac{s-1}{2}+p}\mathcal{E}^{1}_{N_{0}}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\\
\lesssim\;&\int_{0}^{t}\!\!\left[\varepsilon^{1+s}(1+\tau)^{\frac{s+1}{2}+p}\mathcal{D}^{1}_{N_{0}}(\tau)\right]^{\frac{1+s}{3+s}}\!\!(1+\tau)^{(\frac{s+1}{2}+p)\frac{2}{3+s}-1}\!\!\left\{{\mathcal{E}}_{N_{0}+\frac{1+s}{2}}(t)+{\mathcal{E}}_{-s}(t)\right\}^{\frac{2}{3+s}}\mathrm{d}\tau\\
\lesssim\;&\eta\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon^{1+s}(1+\tau)^{\frac{1+s}{2}+p}\mathcal{D}^{1}_{N_{0}}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau+\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\left\{{\mathcal{E}}_{N_{0}+\frac{1+s}{2}}(\tau)+{\mathcal{E}}_{-s}(\tau)\right\}\!\!\int_{0}^{t}(1+\tau)^{-1+p}\mathrm{d}\tau\\
\lesssim\;&\eta\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon^{1+s}(1+\tau)^{\frac{1+s}{2}+p}\mathcal{D}^{1}_{N_{0}}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau+\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\left\{{\mathcal{E}}_{N_{0}+\frac{1+s}{2}}(\tau)+{\mathcal{E}}_{-s}(\tau)\right\}(1+t)^{p}.\end{split}

(3.13)

For the third term on the right-hand side of (
3.12
), we obtain from the
a priori
assumption (
2.2
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\varepsilon^{1+s}\int_{0}^{t}(1+\tau)^{\frac{1+s}{2}+p}\|\nabla_{x}B^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{H^{N_{0}-1}_{x}}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\lesssim\;&\left[X(t)\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{s}\int_{0}^{t}(1+\tau)^{\frac{s}{2}+p}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\\
\lesssim\;&\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{s}\int_{0}^{t}(1+\tau)^{\frac{s}{2}+p}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau.\end{split}

(3.14)

As a consequence, plugging (
3.13
) and (
3.14
) into (
3.12
), and choosing
\eta
small enough, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\left\{\varepsilon^{1+s}(1+t)^{\frac{1+s}{2}}\mathcal{E}^{1}_{N_{0}}(t)\right\}+(1+t)^{-p}\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon^{1+s}(1+\tau)^{\frac{1+s}{2}+p}\mathcal{D}^{1}_{N_{0}}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\\
\lesssim\;&\mathcal{E}_{N}(0)+\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\left\{{\mathcal{E}}_{N_{0}+\frac{1+s}{2}}(\tau)+{\mathcal{E}}_{-s}(\tau)\right\}+\delta_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{s}\int_{0}^{t}(1+\tau)^{\frac{s}{2}+p}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{N_{0}}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau.\end{split}

(3.15)

Therefore, a suitable linear combination of (
3.11
)
and (
3.15
) yields the desired estimate (
3.3
).
This completes the proof of Proposition
3.1
.
∎

3.2.
Proof of Global Existence

With Proposition
2.1
, Proposition
2.4
, Proposition
2.5
, Proposition
2.8
and Proposition
3.1
in hand, we are now in the position to complete the proof of Theorem

1.1
and verify the
a priori
assumption (
2.2
).

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

Collecting (
2.30
), (
2.35
) and (
2.46
), then choosing
\delta_{0}
small enough and using the relation
\frac{1+s}{2}(l_{2}-l_{1})=\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}(N-1)=\sigma_{N-1,N-1}
, we find

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\Big[{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)+{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}_{N,l_{2}}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{-s}(t)+\varepsilon\mathcal{E}^{-s}_{int}(t)\Big]+{\overline{\mathcal{D}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)+{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}}_{N,l_{2}}(t)+\mathcal{D}_{-s}(t)\\
\lesssim\;&\delta_{0}(1+t)^{-\sigma_{N-1,N-1}}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq N-1,|\alpha|\geq 1}\!\!\!\!\big\|\widetilde{w}_{l_{2}}(|\alpha|-1,|\beta|+1)\nabla_{v}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\big\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\\
\;&+\mathcal{D}_{N}(t)+\eta\varepsilon^{2}(1+t)^{-(1+\vartheta)}\|\nabla_{x}^{N}E^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\\[2.84526pt]
\lesssim\;&\delta_{0}\sum_{n\leq N-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{l_{2}}^{(N)}(t)+\mathcal{D}_{N}(t)+\eta\varepsilon^{2}(1+t)^{-(1+\vartheta)}\|\nabla_{x}^{N}E^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2},\end{split}

which further implies

\displaystyle\begin{split}\;&\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\Big[{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)+{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}_{N,l_{2}}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{-s}(t)+\varepsilon\mathcal{E}^{-s}_{int}(t)\Big]+{\overline{\mathcal{D}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)+{\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}}_{N,l_{2}}(t)+\mathcal{D}_{-s}(t)\\
\lesssim\;&\mathcal{D}_{N}(t)+\eta\varepsilon^{2}(1+t)^{-(1+\vartheta)}\|\nabla_{x}^{N}E^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}.\end{split}

(3.16)

Therefore, a proper linear combination of (
2.5
)
and (
3.16
) gives us that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\Big\{\mathcal{E}_{N}(t)+{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)+{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}_{N,l_{2}}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{-s}(t)\Big\}\lesssim\;&\eta\varepsilon^{2}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\{\mathcal{E}_{N}(t)\right\}.\end{split}

(3.17)

Combined with (
3.3
), the estimate (
3.17
) helps us to conclude

\displaystyle\begin{split}X(t)\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{N}(0)+{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(0)+{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}_{N,l_{2}}(0)+\mathcal{E}_{-s}(0)\end{split}

(3.18)

holds for any
0\leq t\leq T
, as long as
\mathcal{E}_{N}(0)+{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(0)+{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}_{N,l_{2}}(0)+\mathcal{E}_{-s}(0)
is sufficiently small.
Furthermore, (
3.18
) further implies the
a priori
assumption (
2.2
).

The rest is to prove the local existence and uniqueness of solutions in terms of the energy norm

\mathcal{E}_{N}(t)+{\overline{\mathcal{E}}}_{N-1,l_{1}}(t)+{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}}_{N,l_{2}}(t)+\mathcal{E}_{-s}(t)

and the non-negativity of
F^{\varepsilon}=\mu+\varepsilon\mu^{1/2}f^{\varepsilon}
. One can use the iteration method with the iterating sequence
\left[f_{n}^{\varepsilon},E^{\varepsilon}_{n},B^{\varepsilon}_{n}\right]

(n\geq 0)
on the system

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\partial_{t}f_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}v\cdot\nabla_{x}{f}_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}-E_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}\cdot v\mu^{1/2}+\dfrac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}L{f}_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}\\[8.53581pt]
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad=-\left(\varepsilon E_{n}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B^{\varepsilon}_{n}\right)\cdot\nabla_{v}{f}_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}E_{n}^{\varepsilon}\cdot v{f}_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Gamma\left({f}_{n}^{\varepsilon},{f}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\right),\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}_{n+1}-\nabla_{x}\times B^{\varepsilon}_{n+1}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v\mu^{1/2}f^{\varepsilon}_{n+1}{\mathrm{d}}v,\qquad\;\nabla_{x}\cdot E^{\varepsilon}_{n+1}=\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mu^{1/2}f^{\varepsilon}_{n+1}{\mathrm{d}}v,\\[8.53581pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}B_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}+\nabla_{x}\times E^{\varepsilon}_{n+1}=0,\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\qquad\;\;\;\nabla_{x}\cdot B^{\varepsilon}_{n+1}=0,\\[8.53581pt]
\displaystyle f_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}(0,x,v)=f^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x,v)\quad E^{\varepsilon}_{n+1}(0,x)=E^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x),\quad B^{\varepsilon}_{n+1}(0,x)=B^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x).\end{array}\right.

The details of proof are omitted for brevity; see
[
21
,
30
]
. Therefore, the global existence of solutions follows with the help of the continuity argument, and the estimate (
1.25
) and (
1.26
) hold by the definition of
X(t)
in (
2.1
). This completes the proof of Theorem
1.1
.
∎

4.
Limit to the Incompressible NSFM System

In this section, our goal is to derive the incompressible NSFM system from the VMB system (
1.2
) as
\varepsilon\to 0
. The proof is similar to that one in
[
30
]
, but here we provide a full proof for completeness.

4.1.
Limits from the Energy Estimate

Based on Theorem
1.1
, the system (
1.2
)
admits a global solution
f^{\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}L^{2}_{v})
and
\left[E^{\varepsilon},\,B^{\varepsilon}\right]\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})
, so there exists a positive constant
C
which is independent of
\varepsilon
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\left\{\left\|f^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|^{2}_{H^{N}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\left\|E^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|^{2}_{H^{N}_{x}}+\left\|B^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|^{2}_{H^{N}_{x}}\right\}\leq C,

(4.1)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}){f}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\|^{2}_{H^{N}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\leq C\varepsilon^{2}.

(4.2)

From the energy bound (
4.1
), there exist
f\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}L^{2}_{v})
and
\nabla_{x}\phi\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N+1}_{x})
such that

\displaystyle f^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\to f\quad\qquad\;\text{~~weakly}\!-\!*~\text{in}~L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}L^{2}_{v}),

(4.3)

\displaystyle E^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\to E\qquad\quad\text{~~weakly}\!-\!*~\text{in}~L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}),

(4.4)

\displaystyle B^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\to B\qquad\quad\text{~~weakly}\!-\!*~\text{in}~L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})

(4.5)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. We still employ the original notation of sequences to denote the subsequences for convenience,
although the limit may hold for some subsequences.
From the dissipation bound (
4.2
), we deduce that

\displaystyle(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\to 0\;\;\;\text{~~strongly}{\text{~in}~L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}L^{2}_{v})}

(4.6)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. We thereby deduce from the convergences (
4.3
) and (
4.6
) that

\displaystyle(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f=0.

(4.7)

This implies that there exist functions
\rho,u,\theta\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{2}_{x})
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}f(t,x,v)=\Big[\rho(t,x)+v\cdot u(t,x)+\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{2}\theta(t,x)\Big]{\mu}^{1/2}.\end{split}

(4.8)

Next, we introduce the following fluid variables

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\rho^{\varepsilon}:=\left\langle f^{\varepsilon},\mu^{1/2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}},\;\;\;\;u^{\varepsilon}:=\left\langle f^{\varepsilon},v\mu^{1/2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}},\;\;\;\;\theta^{\varepsilon}:=\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon},\Big(\frac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1\Big)\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}.\end{split}

(4.9)

Multiplying the first equation of (
1.2
) by
\mu^{1/2}
,
v\mu^{1/2}
,
(\frac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)\mu^{1/2}
respectively and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, we derive that
\rho^{\varepsilon}
,
u^{\varepsilon}
and
\theta^{\varepsilon}
obey the local conservation laws

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot u^{\varepsilon}=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}(\rho^{\varepsilon}+\theta^{\varepsilon})-E^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot\left\langle\widehat{A}(v){\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}},L(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\varepsilon\rho^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}+u_{\varepsilon}\times B^{\varepsilon},\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon}+\frac{2}{3\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot u^{\varepsilon}+\frac{2}{3\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot\left\langle\widehat{B}(v){\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}},L(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\frac{2\varepsilon}{3}u^{\varepsilon}\cdot E^{\varepsilon},\end{array}\right.

(4.10)

where

A(v)=v\otimes v-\frac{|v|^{2}}{3}\mathbb{I}_{3\times 3},~B(v)=v\Big(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\frac{5}{2}\Big),

\widehat{A}(v)
is such that
{L}\big(\widehat{A}(v)\mu^{1/2}\big)=A(v)\mu^{1/2}
with
\widehat{A}(v)\mu^{1/2}\in\mathrm{N}^{\bot}({L})
,

\widehat{B}(v)
is such that
{L}\big(\widehat{B}(v)\mu^{1/2}\big)

=B(v)\mu^{1/2}
with
\widehat{B}(v)\mu^{1/2}\in\mathrm{N}^{\bot}({L})
.

From the second and third equations of (
1.2
) and the definition of
\rho^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon}
in (
4.9
), we also find the equations of
E^{\varepsilon}
and
B^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}-\nabla_{x}\times B^{\varepsilon}=-u^{\varepsilon},\quad\qquad\;\,\,\nabla_{x}\cdot E^{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon\rho^{\varepsilon},\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}B^{\varepsilon}+\nabla_{x}\times E^{\varepsilon}=0,\qquad\qquad\;\;\;\nabla_{x}\cdot B^{\varepsilon}=0.\end{array}\right.

Further, via the definitions of
\rho^{\varepsilon}
,
u^{\varepsilon}
and
\theta^{\varepsilon}
in (
4.9
) and the uniform energy bound (
4.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\Big\{\|\rho^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{N}_{x}}+\|u^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{N}_{x}}+\|\theta^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{N}_{x}}\Big\}\leq C.

(4.13)

From the convergence (
4.3
) and the limit function
f(t,x,v)
given in (
4.8
), we thereby deduce the following convergences

\displaystyle\rho^{\varepsilon}=\left\langle f^{\varepsilon},\mu^{1/2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\to\left\langle f,\mu^{1/2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\rho,~

(4.14)

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}=\left\langle f^{\varepsilon},v\mu^{1/2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\to\left\langle f,v\mu^{1/2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=u,~

(4.15)

\displaystyle\theta^{\varepsilon}=\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon},\Big(\frac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1\Big){\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\to\Big\langle f,\Big(\frac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1\Big){\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\theta,

(4.16)

\text{weakly}\!-\!*~\text{in}~L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+},H^{N}_{x})
as
\varepsilon\to 0
.

4.2.
Convergences to the Limiting System

In this subsection, we deduce the incompressible NSFM system from the local conservation laws (
4.10
), (
4.1
), the convergences (
4.3
)–(
4.6
) and (
4.14
)–(
4.16
) obtained in the previous subsection.

4.2.1.
Incompressibility and Boussinesq relation.

From the first equation of (
4.10
) and the energy uniform bound (
4.13
), it is easy to deduce

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\cdot u^{\varepsilon}=-\varepsilon\partial_{t}\rho^{\varepsilon}\to 0\;\;\;\text{~~in the sense of distributions}

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Combining with the convergence (
4.15
), we get

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\cdot u=0.

(4.17)

The second equation of (
4.10
) yields

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}(\rho^{\varepsilon}+\theta^{\varepsilon})=-\varepsilon\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}-\nabla_{x}\cdot\left\langle\widehat{A}(v){\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}},L(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}+\varepsilon^{2}\rho^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon u^{\varepsilon}\times B^{\varepsilon}.

The bound (
4.13
) implies

\varepsilon\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon}\to 0\;\;\;\text{~~in the sense of distributions}

as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
With the aid of the self-adjointness of
{L}
, we derive from the dissipation bound (
4.2
) and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\Big\|\nabla_{x}\cdot\left\langle\widehat{A}(v)\sqrt{\mu},L(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\Big\|_{H^{N-1}_{x}}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\!=\;&\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big\|\nabla_{x}\cdot\Big\langle{{A}}(v)\mu^{1/2},{(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}}\Big\rangle\Big\|_{H^{N-1}_{x}}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\\
\leq\;&C\int_{0}^{\infty}\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{H^{N}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\\
\leq\;&C\varepsilon^{2}.\end{split}

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\cdot\left\langle\widehat{A}(v)\sqrt{\mu},L(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\to 0\;\;\;\text{~~strongly}{\text{~in}~L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{x})}

as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
Moreover, the uniform energy bounds (
4.1
) and (
4.13
) reveal that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\left\|\varepsilon^{2}\rho^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon u_{\varepsilon}\times B^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{H^{N}_{x}}\leq C\varepsilon,

which means that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\rho^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon u_{\varepsilon}\times B^{\varepsilon}\to 0\;\;\;\text{~~strongly}{\text{~in}~L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})}

as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
In summary, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}(\rho^{\varepsilon}+\theta^{\varepsilon})\to 0\;\;\;\text{~~in the sense of distributions}

(4.18)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
Therefore, combining the convergences (
4.14
), (
4.16
) and (
4.18
), we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}(\rho+\theta)=0.

From the integrability of functions, we can deduce the Boussinesq relation

\displaystyle\rho+\theta=0.

(4.19)

4.2.2.
Convergences of
\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}
,
\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}
,
n^{\varepsilon}
,
E^{\varepsilon}
,
B^{\varepsilon}
.

First of all, we introduce the following Aubin–Lions–Simon Theorem, a fundamental result of compactness in the study of nonlinear evolution problems, which can be referred in
[
6
,
42
]
.

Lemma 4.1

(Aubin–Lions–Simon Theorem)
.

Let
B_{0}\subset B_{1}\subset B_{2}
be three Banach spaces. We assume that the embedding of
B_{1}
in
B_{2}
is continuous and the embedding of
B_{0}
in
B_{1}
is compact. Let
p,r
be such that
1\leq p,r\leq\infty
. For
T>0
, we define

\displaystyle E_{p,r}=\Big\{u\in L^{p}(0,T;B_{0}),\partial_{t}u\in L^{r}(0,T;B_{2})\Big\}.

(1)

If
p<+\infty
, the embedding of
E_{p,r}
in
L^{p}(0,T;B_{1})
is compact.

(2)

If
p=+\infty
and if
r>1
, the embedding of
E_{p,r}
in
C(0,T;B_{1})
is compact.

Now we consider the convergence of
\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}
. It follows from the first equation and the third equation of
(
4.10
)
that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)+\frac{2}{5\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot\left\langle\widehat{B}(v){\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}},L(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\frac{2}{5}\varepsilon u^{\varepsilon}\cdot E^{\varepsilon}.

(4.20)

Thereby, by utilizing the equation (
4.20
), the energy bound (
4.1
), the dissipation bound (
4.2
), one has

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big\|\partial_{t}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{N-1}_{x})}\\
=\;&\Big\|\frac{2}{5}\varepsilon u^{\varepsilon}\cdot E^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot\langle B(v)\mu^{1/2},(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\rangle\Big\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{N-1}_{x})}\\
\leq\;&C\varepsilon\left\|u^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})}\left\|E^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H_{x}^{N})}+\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\left\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}){f}^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}L^{2}_{v}(\nu))}\\
\leq\;&C\end{split}

(4.21)

for any
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
. Further, we can derive from the energy bound (
4.13
) that

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{N}_{x})}\leq C

(4.22)

for any
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
. Notice the fact that

\displaystyle H^{N}\hookrightarrow H^{N-1}_{loc}\hookrightarrow H^{N-1}_{loc},

(4.23)

where the embedding of
H^{N}
in
H^{N-1}_{loc}
is compact by the Rellich–Kondrachov Theorem (see Theorem 6.3 of
[
1
]
, for instance) and the embedding of
H^{N-1}_{loc}
in
H^{N-1}_{loc}
is naturally continuous. Then, from Aubin–Lions–Simon Theorem in Lemma
4.1
, the bounds (
4.21
), (
4.22
) and the embedding (
4.23
), we deduce that there exists a function
\widetilde{\theta}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})
such that

\displaystyle\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\to\widetilde{\theta}\;\;\;\text{~~strongly}{\text{~in}~C(0,T;H^{N-1}_{loc})}

for any
T>0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Combining with the convergences (
4.14
) and (
4.16
), we deduce that
\widetilde{\theta}=\frac{3}{5}\theta-\frac{2}{5}\rho
. Moreover, it follows that
\widetilde{\theta}=\theta
from the Boussinesq relation (
4.19
) and
\theta=\frac{3}{5}\theta-\frac{2}{5}\rho+\frac{2}{5}(\rho+\theta)
. As a result, we obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\to\theta\;\;\;\text{~~strongly}{\text{~in}~C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})}

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, where
\theta\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})
. Noticing that
\theta^{\varepsilon}=\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}+\frac{2}{5}(\rho^{\varepsilon}+\theta^{\varepsilon})
and combining with the convergence (
4.16
), one has

\displaystyle\rho^{\varepsilon}+\theta^{\varepsilon}\to 0\;\;\;\text{weakly}\!-\!*\text{~in~}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}),\;\text{~~strongly}\text{~in}~C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})

as
\varepsilon\to 0
.

Next, we consider the convergence of
\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}
, where
\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}
is the Leray projection operator defined by
\mathcal{P}=I-\nabla_{x}\Delta_{x}^{-1}\nabla_{x}\cdot
. By taking
\mathcal{P}
on the second equation of (
4.10
), we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{P}\nabla_{x}\cdot\left\langle\widehat{A}(v){\mu}^{\frac{1}{2}},L(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\mathcal{P}(E^{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon\rho^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}+u_{\varepsilon}\times B^{\varepsilon}).

(4.24)

Hence, it follows from the equation (
4.24
), the uniform dissipation bound (
4.2
), the uniform energy bound (
4.13
) and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{N-1}_{x})}\\
\lesssim\>&\|E^{\varepsilon}\!+\!\varepsilon\rho^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}\!+\!u_{\varepsilon}\times B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{N-1}_{x})}+\Big\|\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot\Big\langle{A}(v)\mu^{1/2},(\mathbf{I}\!-\!\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{N-1}_{x})}\\
\lesssim\>&\left\|E^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})}+\varepsilon\left\|\rho^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})}\left\|E^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})}\!+\!\left\|u^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};\!H^{N}_{x})}\left\|B^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};\!H^{N}_{x})}\\
\;&+\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\left\|(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}N^{s}_{\gamma})}\\
\lesssim\;&C\end{split}

(4.25)

for any
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
. Furthermore, we can derive from the definition of
u^{\varepsilon}
in (
4.9
) and the uniform energy bound (
4.1
) that for any
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{N}_{x})}\leq C.

(4.26)

Then, from Aubin–Lions–Simon Theorem in Lemma
4.1
, the bounds (
4.25
), (
4.26
) and the embedding (
4.23
), we deduce that there exists a function
\widetilde{u}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\to\widetilde{u}\;\;\;\text{~~strongly}{\text{~in}~C(0,T;H^{N-1}_{loc})}

for any
T>0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Moreover, from the convergence (
4.15
) and the incompressibility (
4.17
), we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{u}=\mathcal{P}u=u.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\to u\;\;\;\text{weakly}\!-\!*~\text{in~}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}),\;\text{~~strongly}{\text{~in}~C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})}

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, where
u\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})
. Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{\bot}u^{\varepsilon}\to 0\;\;\;\text{weakly}\!-\!*~\text{in~}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})\;\text{~~strongly}\text{~in}~C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Here,
\mathcal{P}^{\bot}:=I-\mathcal{P}
.

Finally, we consider the convergences of
E^{\varepsilon}
and
B^{\varepsilon}
. From (
4.1
) and the uniform bounds (
4.1
), (
4.13
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\|\partial_{t}E^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{N-1}_{x})}\;&=\left\|\nabla_{x}\times B^{\varepsilon}-u^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{N-1}_{x})}\leq C,\\
\left\|\partial_{t}B^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{N-1}_{x})}\;&=\left\|\nabla_{x}\times E^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{N-1}_{x})}\leq C\end{split}

(4.27)

for any
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
. Furthermore, from the bound (
4.1
), we deduce that for any
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1

\displaystyle\left\|E^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{N}_{x})}+\left\|B^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{N}_{x})}\leq C.

(4.28)

Then, from Aubin–Lions–Simon Theorem in Lemma
4.1
and the bounds (
4.27
),
(
4.28
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\left[E^{\varepsilon},B^{\varepsilon}\right]\to\left[E,B\right]\;\;\;\text{weakly}\!-\!*~\text{in~}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}),\;\text{~~strongly}{\text{~in}~C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})}

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, where
\left[E,B\right]\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})
.

In summary, we have deduced the following convergences:

\displaystyle\!\!\!\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon},\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon},E^{\varepsilon},B^{\varepsilon}\Big)\to(\theta,u,E,B)\;\;\;\text{weakly}\!-\!*~\text{in~}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}),\;

(4.29)

strongly in
C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})
as
\varepsilon\to 0
, where
(\theta,u,E,B)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})
and

\displaystyle\left(\rho^{\varepsilon}+\theta^{\varepsilon},\mathcal{P}^{\bot}u\right)\to(0,0)\;\;\;\text{weakly}\!-\!*~\text{in~}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}),\;\text{~~strongly}\text{~in}~C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})

(4.30)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
.

4.2.3.
Equations of
\rho
,
u
,
\theta
,
E
and
B

We first calculate the term

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\langle\widehat{\Xi}(v)\mu^{1/2},L(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}},

where
\Xi=A
or
B
.
Following the standard formal derivations of fluid dynamic limits of the Boltzmann equation (see
[
4
]
for instance), we obtain

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Big\langle\widehat{A}(v)\mu^{1/2},L(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\;

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}\otimes u^{\varepsilon}-\frac{|u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}}{3}\mathbb{I}_{3\times 3}-\nu\Sigma(u^{\varepsilon})-R^{\varepsilon}_{A},

(4.31)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Big\langle\widehat{B}(v)\mu^{1/2},L(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}=\;

\displaystyle\frac{5}{2}u^{\varepsilon}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{5}{2}\kappa\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon}-R^{\varepsilon}_{B},

(4.32)

where

\displaystyle\nu:=\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{10}\Big\langle\widehat{A}(v)\mu^{1/2},A(v)\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}},

(4.33)

\displaystyle\kappa:=\;

\displaystyle\frac{2}{15}\Big\langle\widehat{B}(v)\mu^{1/2},B(v)\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}},

(4.34)

\displaystyle\Sigma(u^{\varepsilon}):=\;

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}u^{\varepsilon}+({\nabla_{x}u^{\varepsilon}})^{T}-\frac{2}{3}\nabla_{x}\cdot u^{\varepsilon}\mathbb{I}_{3\times 3},

\displaystyle R^{\varepsilon}_{\Xi}:=\;

\displaystyle\left\langle\hat{\Xi}(v)\sqrt{\mu},\;\;\varepsilon\partial_{t}f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}}+\left\langle\hat{\Xi}(v)\sqrt{\mu},\;\;v\cdot\nabla_{x}(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon}\right\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\Big\langle\hat{\Xi}(v)\sqrt{\mu},\;\;\varepsilon\left(\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}+v\times B^{\varepsilon}\right)\cdot\frac{\nabla_{v}\left({\sqrt{\mu}f^{\varepsilon}}\right)}{{\sqrt{\mu}}}\Big\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}}-\left\langle\hat{\Xi}(v)\sqrt{\mu},\;\;\Gamma((\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon})\right\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}}

\displaystyle-\left\langle\hat{\Xi}(v)\sqrt{\mu},\;\;\Gamma(\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon},(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P})f^{\varepsilon})\right\rangle_{L_{v}^{2}}

with
\Xi=A
or
B
.

The relation (
4.31
), the decomposition
u^{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{P}^{\bot}u^{\varepsilon}
and the equation (
4.24
) yield that

\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{P}\nabla_{x}\cdot(\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon})-\nu\Delta_{x}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{P}(E^{\varepsilon}+u^{\varepsilon}\times B^{\varepsilon})+R^{\varepsilon}_{u},

(4.35)

where
R^{\varepsilon}_{u}
is given by
