R^{\varepsilon}_{u}:=\varepsilon\rho^{\varepsilon}E^{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{P}\nabla_{x}\cdot R^{\varepsilon}_{A}-\mathcal{P}\nabla_{x}\cdot(\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u^{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{P}^{\bot}u^{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u^{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u^{\varepsilon}).

(4.36)

The relation (
4.32
), the decomposition
u^{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{P}^{\bot}u^{\varepsilon}
and the equation (
4.20
) yield that

\partial_{t}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)+\nabla_{x}\cdot\Big[\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big]-\kappa\Delta_{x}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)=R^{\varepsilon}_{\theta},

(4.37)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}R^{\varepsilon}_{\theta}:=\;&\frac{2}{5}\nabla_{x}\cdot R^{\varepsilon}_{B}-\frac{2}{5}\nabla_{x}\cdot\big[\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}(\rho^{\varepsilon}+\theta^{\varepsilon})\big]-\frac{2}{5}\nabla_{x}\cdot\big[\mathcal{P}^{\bot}u^{\varepsilon}(\rho^{\varepsilon}+\theta^{\varepsilon})\big]\\
&-\nabla_{x}\cdot\Big[\mathcal{P}^{\bot}u^{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big]+\frac{2}{5}\kappa\Delta_{x}(\rho^{\varepsilon}+\theta^{\varepsilon})+\frac{2}{5}\varepsilon E^{\varepsilon}\cdot u^{\varepsilon}.\end{split}

(4.38)

Now, we take the limit from the equation (
4.35
) to obtain the
u
-equation of (
1.32
). For any given
T>0
, choose a vector-valued test function
\Psi(t,x)\in C^{1}(0,T;C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
with
\nabla_{x}\cdot\Psi=0
,
\Psi(0,x)=\Psi_{0}(x)\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
\Psi(t,x)=0
for
t\geq T^{\prime}
, where
T^{\prime}<T
. Multiplying (
4.35
) by
\Psi(t,x)
and integrating over
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\Psi(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\\
=\;&-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}(0,x)\cdot\Psi(0,x){\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\Psi(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\\
=\;&-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{P}\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon}_{0},v\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\cdot\Psi_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\Psi(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.\end{split}

The initial conditions in Theorem
1.2
and the convergence (
4.29
) give us that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{P}\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon}_{0},v\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\cdot\Psi_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x\to\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{P}\Big\langle f_{0},v\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\cdot\Psi_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{P}u_{0}\cdot\Psi_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x

and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\Psi(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\to\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u\cdot\partial_{t}\Psi(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. That is, we deduce

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\Psi(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\to-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{P}u_{0}\cdot\Psi_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u\cdot\partial_{t}\Psi(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

(4.39)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
With the aid of the bounds (
4.1
), (
4.2
), (
4.13
) and the convergences (
4.29
)–(
4.30
), we show the following convergences

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{P}\nabla_{x}\cdot(\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon})\to\mathcal{P}\nabla_{x}\cdot(u\otimes u)\quad\text{strongly in}~\;C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-2}_{loc}),\\[5.69054pt]
\nu\Delta_{x}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\to\nu\Delta_{x}u\;\;\;\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\quad\text{~~in the sense of distributions},\\[5.69054pt]
\mathcal{P}(E^{\varepsilon}+u^{\varepsilon}\times B^{\varepsilon})\to\mathcal{P}(E+u\times B)\;\;\;\;\;\,\,\text{strongly in}~\;C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H_{loc}^{N-1}),\\[5.69054pt]
R^{\varepsilon}_{u}\to 0\;\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\text{~~in the sense of distributions}\end{array}\right.

(4.40)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Therefore, the convergences (
4.39
), (
4.40
) and the incompressibility (
4.17
) imply that
u\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})
subjects to

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\partial_{t}u+\mathcal{P}\nabla_{x}\cdot(u\otimes u)-\nu\Delta_{x}u=\mathcal{P}(E+u\times B),\\[5.69054pt]
\nabla_{x}\cdot u=0\end{array}\right.

(4.41)

with initial data
u(0,x)=\mathcal{P}u_{0}(x)
.

Next, we take the limit from the equation (
4.37
) to gain the
\theta
-equation of (
1.32
). For any given
T>0
, choose a test function
\Phi(t,x)\in C^{1}(0,T;C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
with
\Phi(0,x)=\Phi_{0}(x)\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
\Phi(t,x)=0
for
t\geq T^{\prime}
, where
T^{\prime}<T
. Then from the initial conditions in Theorem
1.2
and the convergence (
4.29
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)\Phi(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\\
=\;&-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon}_{0},(\frac{|v|^{2}}{5}-1)\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle\Phi_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)\partial_{t}\Phi(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\\
\to\;&-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big\langle f_{0},(\frac{|v|^{2}}{5}-1)\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle\Phi_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\theta(t,x)\partial_{t}\Phi(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\\
=\;&-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta_{0}-\frac{2}{5}\rho_{0}\Big)\Phi_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\theta(t,x)\partial_{t}\Phi(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\end{split}

(4.42)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Furthermore, with the help of the bounds (
4.1
)–(
4.2
), (
4.13
) and the convergences (
4.29
)–(
4.30
), we derive the following convergences

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\nabla_{x}\cdot\Big[\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big]\to\nabla_{x}\cdot(u\theta)\quad\,\,\text{strongly in}~\;C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H_{loc}^{N-2}),\\[5.69054pt]
\kappa\Delta_{x}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)\to\kappa\Delta_{x}\theta\;\;\;\;\,\,\qquad\qquad\text{~~in the sense of distributions},\\[5.69054pt]
R^{\varepsilon}_{\theta}\to 0\,\;\;\;\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\qquad\quad\quad\quad\text{in the sense of distributions}\end{array}\right.

(4.43)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
Therefore, the convergences (
4.42
), (
4.43
) and the incompressibility (
4.17
) imply that that
\theta\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})
obeys

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta+u\cdot\nabla_{x}\theta-\kappa\Delta_{x}\theta=0

(4.44)

with the initial data
\theta(0,x)=\frac{3}{5}\theta_{0}(x)-\frac{2}{5}\rho_{0}(x)
.

Finally, the convergence (
4.29
) and the equation
(
4.1
) show that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\partial_{t}E-\nabla_{x}\times B=-u,\quad\qquad\;\,\,\,\nabla_{x}\cdot E=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}B+\nabla_{x}\times E=0,\qquad\qquad\;\;\;\nabla_{x}\cdot B=0\end{array}\right.

with the initial date
E(0,x)=E_{0}(x)
and
B(0,x)=B_{0}(x)
.

4.2.4.
Summarization

Collecting all above convergence results, we have shown that

\displaystyle\left(\rho,u,\theta,E,B\right)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc}),

and the following incompressible NSFM system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\partial_{t}u+u\cdot\nabla_{x}u-\nu\Delta_{x}u+\nabla_{x}P=E+u\times B,&\nabla_{x}\cdot u=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta+u\cdot\nabla_{x}\theta-\kappa\Delta_{x}\theta=0,&\rho+\theta=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}E-\nabla_{x}\times B=-u,&\nabla_{x}\cdot E=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}B+\nabla_{x}\times E=0,&\nabla_{x}\cdot B=0\\[5.69054pt]
\end{array}\right.

(4.45)

with the initial data

\displaystyle\begin{split}u(0,x)=\mathcal{P}u_{0}(x),\;\;\theta(0,x)=\frac{3}{5}\theta_{0}(x)-\frac{2}{5}\rho_{0}(x),\;\;E(0,x)=E_{0}(x),\;\;B(0,x)=B_{0}(x).&\end{split}

Actually, by mollifying the system (
4.45
) and combining the local existence theory, the standard energy method and the continuity argument, we can show that
(\rho,u,\theta,E,B)\in C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H_{x}^{N})
, cf. Chapter 3.2 in
[
38
]
, and then (
1.31
) holds true. Moreover, by approximating
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}
by bounded
region
[
9
]
and the unform bounds of functions and their limits proved above, the convergences in (
1.29
) are thus verified.
This completes the proof of Theorem
1.2
.

Appendix A
Appendix

For convenience, we list the some basic results and inequalities to be used throughout this paper.

Take that
w_{\ell}\equiv w_{\ell}(v)=\langle v\rangle^{\ell}e^{\frac{q\langle v\rangle^{2}}{(1+t)^{\vartheta}}}
.
The first lemma concerns the coercivity estimate on the linearized operator
L
, proved by
[
16
,
23
]
.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
.

(1)

There exists a constant
C\geq 0
such that the uniform coercive lower bound estimate

\left\langle Lf,w_{\ell}^{2}f\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\gtrsim\left|w_{\ell}f\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}-C\left|f\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}

(A.1)

holds for any
\ell\in\mathbb{R}
.

(2)

For
|\beta|\geq 1
be a multi-index, we have

\left\langle\partial_{\beta}Lf,w_{\ell}^{2}\partial_{\beta}f\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\gtrsim\left|w_{\ell}\partial_{\beta}f\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}-\eta\sum_{|\beta^{\prime}|<|\beta|}\left|w_{\ell}\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}f\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}-C_{\eta}\left|f\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}

(A.2)

for any
\ell\in\mathbb{R}
and small
\eta>0
.

The following lemma focus on the estimate of the nonlinear collision operator
\Gamma
, which can be found in
[
16
]
.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
0\leq\gamma\leq 1
.

(1)

There holds

\displaystyle|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Gamma(f,g)|_{L^{2}_{v}}\lesssim|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}f|_{L^{2}_{v}}|g|_{L^{2}_{v}}+|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g|_{L^{2}_{v}}|f|_{L^{2}_{v}}.

(A.3)

(2)

For
\alpha\geq 0
and
\beta\geq 0
be two multi-indexs. Then, there holds

\displaystyle\begin{split}\!\!\!\;&\left\langle\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}\Gamma(f,g),w_{\ell}^{2}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}h\right\rangle_{L^{2}_{v}}\\
\lesssim\;&\sum\left\{\left|w_{0}\partial_{\beta_{1}}^{\alpha_{1}}f\right|_{L^{2}_{v}}\left|w_{\ell}\partial_{\beta_{2}}^{\alpha_{2}}g\right|_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}+\left|w_{\ell}\partial_{\beta_{1}}^{\alpha_{1}}f\right|_{L^{2}_{v}}\left|w_{0}\partial_{\beta_{2}}^{\alpha_{2}}g\right|_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}\right\}\left|w_{\ell}\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}h\right|_{L^{2}_{v}(\nu)}.\end{split}

(A.4)

where the summation
\sum
is taken over
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}=\alpha
and
\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}\leq\beta
.

In what follows, we present the following Sobolev interpolation inequalities was shown in
[
26
]
.

Lemma A.3
.

Let
2\leq p<\infty
and
k,\ell,m\in\mathbb{R}
. Then for any
f\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{k}_{x}f\|_{L^{p}_{x}}\lesssim\|\nabla^{\ell}_{x}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{\theta}\|\nabla^{m}_{x}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{1-\theta},

(A.5)

where
0\leq\theta\leq 1
and
\ell
satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}-\frac{k}{3}=\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\ell}{3}\right)\theta+\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{m}{3}\right)(1-\theta).

For the case
p=+\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{k}_{x}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|\nabla^{\ell}_{x}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{\theta}\|\nabla^{m}_{x}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{1-\theta},

(A.6)

where
\ell\leq k+1
,
m\geq k+2
,
0\leq\theta\leq 1
and
\ell
satisfies

\displaystyle-\frac{k}{3}=\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\ell}{3}\right)\theta+\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{m}{3}\right)(1-\theta).

If
s\in(0,3)
, then
\Lambda^{-s}
is the Riesz potential operator. The
Hardy–Littlewood–Sobolev theorem implies the following inequality for the Riesz potential operator
\Lambda^{-s}
.

Lemma A.4
.

Let
0<s<3
,
1<p<q<\infty
,
1/q+s/3=1/p
, then

\displaystyle\left\|\Lambda^{-s}f\right\|_{L^{q}_{x}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{p}_{x}}.

(A.7)

Further, based on Lemma
A.3
, we have the following lemma to be used frequently in this article.

Lemma A.5
.

Let
0<s<3/2
. Then there holds

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\frac{12}{3+2s}}_{x}}\lesssim\|\Lambda^{\frac{3}{4}-\frac{s}{2}}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}},\quad\|f\|_{L^{\frac{3}{s}}_{x}}\lesssim\|\Lambda^{\frac{3}{2}-s}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.

(A.8)

Finally, we also use the following Minkowski inequality to interchange the order of a multiple integral, cf.
[
26
]
.

Lemma A.6
.

For
1\leq p\leq q\leq\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L_{x}^{q}L_{v}^{p}}\leq\|f\|_{L_{v}^{p}L_{x}^{q}}.

(A.9)
